) by the following choice of activation function:

\sigma_{*}\left(\boldsymbol{x};\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)=\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right),\qquad\kappa=\sqrt{d},

(19)

where
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and

\mathfrak{Dim}=\left(d,d,d\right)
in this setting (
D_{{\rm in}}=D_{{\rm out}}=D=d
).
The rationale for the choice
\kappa=\sqrt{d}
has been discussed
in Section
2.3.1
. The regularization
\Lambda

represents a
\ell_{2}
-regularized autoencoder:
\Lambda\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{z}\right)=\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}
,
where
\lambda\geq 0
. Here we allow
\lambda
to be dependent on

\mathfrak{Dim}
, but impose a constraint that
\lambda\leq C
for
some immaterial constant
C
that is independent of
\mathfrak{Dim}
.
For simplicity, we have chosen a constant learning rate schedule
\xi\left(\cdot\right)=1

in our autoencoder application; the extension to bounded Lipschitz

\xi
is straightforwards. We consider the following two scenarios:

]

[S.1]

(Setting with ReLU activation)
The
data
\boldsymbol{y}=\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
follows a Gaussian
distribution with the following mean and covariance:

\mathbb{E}\left\{\boldsymbol{x}\right\}=\boldsymbol{0},\qquad\mathbb{E}\left\{\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}=\frac{1}{d}\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\Sigma_{1}^{2},...,\Sigma_{d}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top},

for
\Sigma_{1}\geq...\geq\Sigma_{d}
and
\boldsymbol{R}
an orthogonal
matrix. In this case, let us define

\boldsymbol{\Sigma}=\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\Sigma_{1},...,\Sigma_{d}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}.

We assume
\sigma_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right)=\Sigma_{d}\geq C\kappa_{*}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{2}=\Sigma_{1}\leq C
.
Here
\kappa_{*}>0
depends uniquely on
d
(and in general, may
decay with increasing
d
), and of course,
\kappa_{*}\leq C
. The
activation
\sigma
is the ReLU:
\sigma\left(a\right)=\max\left(0,a\right)
.

[S.2]

(Setting with bounded activation)

The data
\boldsymbol{y}=\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
follows
a Gaussian distribution with the following mean and covariance:

\mathbb{E}\left\{\boldsymbol{x}\right\}=\boldsymbol{0},\qquad\mathbb{E}\left\{\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}=\frac{1}{d}{\rm diag}(\underbrace{\Sigma_{1}^{2},...,\Sigma_{1}^{2}}_{d_{1}\text{ entries}},\underbrace{\Sigma_{2}^{2},...,\Sigma_{2}^{2}}_{d_{2}\text{ entries}}),

where
0<C\leq\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}\leq C
, and
d_{1}=\alpha d
,

d_{2}=\left(1-\alpha\right)d
for some
\alpha\in\left(0,1\right)

such that
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
are positive integers, and
\alpha

does not depend on
\mathfrak{Dim}
. In this case, let us define

\boldsymbol{\Sigma}={\rm diag}(\underbrace{\Sigma_{1},...,\Sigma_{1}}_{d_{1}\text{ entries}},\underbrace{\Sigma_{2},...,\Sigma_{2}}_{d_{2}\text{ entries}}).

The activation
\sigma
is bounded and thrice differentiable with
bounded first two derivatives
\left\|\sigma\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\sigma^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\sigma^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C
,
such that there exist an anti-derivative
\hat{\sigma}_{2}
of
\left|\sigma^{\prime\prime}\right|

with
\left\|\hat{\sigma}_{2}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C
and
an anti-derivative
\hat{\sigma}_{3}
of
\left|\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}\right|

with
\left\|\hat{\sigma}_{3}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C
. For
simplicity, we assume
d_{1},d_{2}>16
. The analysis could be extended
to scenarios where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
is non-diagonal and the
spectrum of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
contains more than two blocks.

In setting
[S.1
, we also recall the two-staged
process as described in Result
2
:

1.

Train an autoencoder with activation of the form (
19
)
and
N
neurons for
t/\epsilon
SGD steps.

2.

Form a set of
M
vectors
\left(\boldsymbol{w}_{i}^{t}\right)_{i\leq M}

such that for each
i\in\left[M\right]
,
\boldsymbol{w}_{i}^{t}=\boldsymbol{w}_{i}^{t}\left(N,t,\epsilon\right)

is drawn independently at random from the set of
N
neurons
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t/\epsilon}\right)_{i\leq N}
.
Construct a new autoencoder with
M
neurons
\left(\boldsymbol{w}_{i}^{t}\right)_{i\leq M}
:

\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{M}^{t}\left(\boldsymbol{x}\right)\equiv\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{M}^{t}\left(\boldsymbol{x};N,t,\epsilon\right)=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\kappa\boldsymbol{w}_{i}^{t}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{w}_{i}^{t},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right).

(20)

In the following, we shall state the main results for each of the
settings (Theorems
13
and
15

in Sections
4.1
and
4.2
respectively).
Their proofs, as well as the proofs for auxiliary results, are presented
in Sections
4.3
-
4.6
.

4.1
Setting with ReLU activation: Main result

We state the main result for the setting with ReLU activation (setting

[S.1
).

Theorem 13
.

Consider setting
[S.1
.
Suppose that the initialization
\rho^{0}=\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},r_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}/d\right)

for a non-negative constant
r_{0}\leq C
and we generate the SGD
initialization
\Theta^{0}=\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{0}\right)_{i\leq N}\sim_{{\rm i.i.d.}}\rho^{0}
.
Given
\delta>1
,
\epsilon_{0}\in\left(0,1\right)
and a finite

T\in\mathbb{N}\epsilon
, assume

\frac{d^{6}\delta^{2}}{\kappa_{*}^{2}}\epsilon\lesssim 1,\qquad\left(\delta^{2}+\log^{5}\left(\frac{NT}{\epsilon}+1\right)\right)\frac{d^{4}}{\kappa_{*}^{2}N}\lesssim 1,

and define

\displaystyle\mathsf{err}\left(N,\epsilon,\delta\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\delta^{2}+\log^{5}\left(\frac{NT}{\epsilon}+1\right)\right)\frac{d^{2}}{N}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\kappa_{*}\delta+\epsilon d^{4}\delta,

\displaystyle\mathsf{prob}\left(N,\delta,\epsilon_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}+\exp\left(Cd\log\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\kappa_{*}}+e\right)-C\frac{N\kappa_{*}^{2}}{d}\right)+\exp\left(-N^{1/8}\right)+\frac{NT}{\epsilon}\exp\left(-C\epsilon_{0}^{1/3}\left(\frac{N}{d^{2}}\right)^{1/6}\right).

The following statements hold:

Properties of trained autoencoders.

For any
1
-Lipschitz function
\phi:\;\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
,
with probability at least
1-C\mathsf{prob}\left(N,\delta,\epsilon_{0}\right)
,
the following properties hold:

\max_{t\in\mathbb{N}\epsilon\cap\left[0,T\right]}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t/\epsilon}\right)-\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left\{\phi\left(\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1,t},...,r_{d,t}\right)\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\}\right|\lesssim\epsilon_{0}+\sqrt{\mathsf{err}\left(N,\epsilon,\delta\right)},

\max_{t\in\mathbb{N}\epsilon\cap\left[0,T\right]}\left|{\cal R}\left(\rho_{N}^{t/\epsilon}\right)-\frac{1}{2d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Sigma_{i}^{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{2}r_{i,t}^{2}\right)^{2}\right|\lesssim d\sqrt{\mathsf{err}\left(N,\epsilon,\delta\right)}+\epsilon_{0},

Here
\boldsymbol{z}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{I}_{d}/d\right)

and we define

r_{i,t}=\sqrt{\frac{\Sigma_{i}^{2}-2\lambda}{0.5r_{0}^{2}\Sigma_{i}^{2}-\left(0.5r_{0}^{2}\Sigma_{i}^{2}-\Sigma_{i}^{2}+2\lambda\right)\exp\left\{-2\left(\Sigma_{i}^{2}-2\lambda\right)t\right\}}}r_{0}.

(In the above, the immaterial constants
C
may depend on
T
and

r_{0}
, but not
N
,
\epsilon
,
d
,
\delta
or
\epsilon_{0}
.)

Two-staged process.

Given a positive integer
M
, perform the two-staged process in (
20
)
to obtain a new autoencoder with
M
neurons
\left(\boldsymbol{w}_{i}^{t}\right)_{i\leq M}
.
Suppose that
M=\mu d
for some
\mu>0
. We then have, for
\epsilon_{0}\in\left(0,1\right)

and
t\geq 0
,

\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|{\cal R}\left(\nu_{M}^{t}\right)-{\cal R}_{*}^{t}\right|\geq\epsilon_{0}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{\mu M}}\right\}\leq C\exp\left(-C\epsilon_{0}^{1/6}\left(1+\frac{1}{\mu}\right)^{-1/6}M^{1/12}\right).

where
\nu_{M}^{t}=\left(1/M\right)\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{M}\delta_{\boldsymbol{w}_{i}^{t}}

and

{\cal R}_{*}^{t}=\frac{1}{2d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Sigma_{i}^{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{2}r_{i,t}^{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{4\mu d^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}r_{i,t}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}r_{i,t}^{2}\Sigma_{i}^{2}.

(In the above, the immaterial constants
C
may depend on
r_{0}
,
but not
M
,
d
,
\delta
,
\epsilon_{0}
,
t
or
\mu
.)

Remark 14
.

In Theorem
13
, a more quantitative statement
for the two-staged process could be made. Here we opt for the limits

N\to\infty
,
\epsilon\to 0
for ease of presentation.

4.2
Setting with bounded activation: Main result

Given an activation
\sigma
, we define
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
on
the domain
\left(a,b\right)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)
:

\displaystyle q_{1}\left(a,b\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\kappa\omega_{11}\sigma\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega_{21}\right)\right\},

(21)

\displaystyle q_{2}\left(a,b\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\kappa\omega_{21}\sigma\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega_{21}\right)\right\},

(22)

in which
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\sim\text{Unif}\left(\mathbb{S}^{d_{1}-1}\right)

and
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\sim\text{Unif}\left(\mathbb{S}^{d_{2}-1}\right)

independently, and
\omega_{11}
and
\omega_{21}
are their respective
first entries. From here onwards, we shall use
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}

and
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}
to indicate these respective random
vectors. For a vector
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we shall
use
\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[1\right]}
to denote a
d_{1}
-dimensional
vector of its first
d_{1}
entries and
\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[2\right]}

to denote a
d_{2}
-dimensional vector of its last
d_{2}
entries.

We state the main result for the setting with bounded activation (setting

[S.2
).

Theorem 15
.

Consider setting
[S.2
.
Suppose that the initialization
\rho^{0}=\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},r_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}/d\right)

for a non-negative constant
r_{0}\leq C
and we generate the SGD
initialization
\Theta^{0}=\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{0}\right)_{i\leq N}\sim_{{\rm i.i.d.}}\rho^{0}
.
Given
\delta>1
,
\epsilon_{0}\in\left(0,1\right)
and a finite

T\in\mathbb{N}\epsilon
, assume

d^{6}\delta^{2}\epsilon\lesssim 1,\qquad\left(\delta^{2}+\log^{5}\left(\frac{NT}{\epsilon}+1\right)\right)\frac{d^{4}}{N}\lesssim 1,

and define

\displaystyle\mathsf{err}\left(N,\epsilon,\delta\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\delta^{2}+\log^{5}\left(\frac{NT}{\epsilon}+1\right)\right)\frac{d^{2}}{N}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\delta+\epsilon d^{4}\delta,

\displaystyle\mathsf{prob}\left(N,\delta,\epsilon_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}+\exp\left(Cd\log\left(d\sqrt{N}+e\right)-CN/d^{2}\right)+\exp\left(-N^{1/8}\right)+\frac{NT}{\epsilon}\exp\left(-C\epsilon_{0}^{1/3}\left(\frac{N}{d^{2}}\right)^{1/6}\right).

Let us also define two non-negative (random) processes
\left(r_{1,t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

and
\left(r_{2,t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
which satisfy the following self-contained
(randomly initialized) ODEs:

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}r_{j,t}

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\rho_{r}^{t}\right)\left[q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,t},\chi_{2}r_{2,t}\right)+\chi_{j}r_{j,t}\partial_{j}q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,t},\chi_{2}r_{2,t}\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{\neg j}\left(\chi,\rho_{r}^{t}\right)\chi_{j}r_{\neg j,t}\partial_{j}q_{\neg j}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,t},\chi_{2}r_{2,t}\right)\right\}-2\lambda r_{j,t},

\displaystyle\rho_{r}^{t}

\displaystyle={\rm Law}\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t}\right),

(23)

for
j=1,2
, and
\neg j=2
if
j=1
,
\neg j=1
if
j=2
. In
the above:

•

q_{1}
and
q_{2}
are functions defined in Eq. (
21
)
and (
22
),

•

the initialization is
r_{1,0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}r_{0}d^{-1/2}Z_{1}

and
r_{2,0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}r_{0}d^{-1/2}Z_{2}
independently,
with
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
being respectively
\chi
-random variables
of degrees of freedom
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
,

•

\chi_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\Sigma_{1}d^{-1/2}Z_{1}
and
\chi_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\Sigma_{2}d^{-1/2}Z_{2}

are two independent random variables, which are also independent of
everything else, and
\chi=\left(\chi_{1},\chi_{2}\right)
,

•

the quantity
\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\rho_{r}^{t}\right)
is defined
as:

\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\rho_{r}^{t}\right)=\int\bar{r}_{j}q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}\bar{r}_{1},\chi_{2}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)-\chi_{j},\qquad j=1,2.

Then for any
1
-Lipschitz function
\phi:\;\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
,
with probability at least
1-C\mathsf{prob}\left(N,\delta,\epsilon_{0}\right)
,

\max_{t\in\mathbb{N}\epsilon\cap\left[0,T\right]}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t/\epsilon}\right)-\int\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\phi\left(\left(\bar{r}_{1}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1},\bar{r}_{2}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\right)\right)\right\}\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\right|\lesssim\epsilon_{0}+\sqrt{\mathsf{err}\left(N,\epsilon,\delta\right)},

\max_{t\in\mathbb{N}\epsilon\cap\left[0,T\right]}\left|{\cal R}\left(\rho_{N}^{t/\epsilon}\right)-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\rho_{r}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}\right|\lesssim d\sqrt{\mathsf{err}\left(N,\epsilon,\delta\right)}+\epsilon_{0}.

(In the above, the immaterial constants
C
may depend on
T
and

r_{0}
, but not
N
,
\epsilon
,
d
,
\delta
or
\epsilon_{0}
.)

4.3
Setting with ReLU activation: Proof of Theorem
13

We prove Theorem
13
. Our proof uses several auxiliary
results, which are stated and proven in Section
4.4
.

Proof of Theorem
13
.

We decompose the proof into several parts.

Proof of the first statement: Properties of trained autoencoders.

The first statement follows from Theorem
7
, Propositions

16
,
17
,

18
,
19
,
20

and
22
. In particular, we have that

\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}=\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\frac{r_{1,t}}{r_{0}},...,\frac{r_{d,t}}{r_{0}}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{0},\qquad\rho^{t}=\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1,t}^{2},...,r_{d,t}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}/d\right)

form the (weakly) unique solution to the ODE (
9
) with
initialization
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{0}\sim\rho^{0}
and
\rho^{0}
.
We also observe that

r_{i,t}\leq\max\left\{r_{0},\sqrt{2\max\left(1-2\lambda/\Sigma_{i}^{2},0\right)}\right\}\leq\max\left\{r_{0},\sqrt{2}\right\}\leq C,

for all
i\in\left[d\right]
and all
t\geq 0
. Furthermore we have
that

\left|\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}r_{i,t}\right|=r_{i,t}\left|0.5\Sigma_{i}^{2}r_{i,t}^{2}-\left(\Sigma_{i}^{2}-2\lambda\right)\right|\leq C,

for all
i\in\left[d\right]
and all
t\geq 0
. These verify Assumption

[A.1
and allow Propositions
16
,

17
and
22

to verify Assumptions
[A.3
and
[A.6
.
Finally, by Stein’s lemma, we have:

\int\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\rho^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1,t}^{2},...,r_{d,t}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x},

and therefore,

\displaystyle{\cal R}\left(\rho^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{x}-\int\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\rho^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{x}-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1,t}^{2},...,r_{d,t}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Sigma_{i}^{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{2}r_{i,t}^{2}\right)^{2}.

This concludes the proof of the first statement.

Proof of the second statement: Two-staged process.

We let
\boldsymbol{z}_{i}=\left(\sqrt{d}/r_{0}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{0}

and hence
\left(\boldsymbol{z}_{i}\right)_{i\leq M}\sim_{{\rm i.i.d.}}\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)
.
We let
\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}
denote the empirical distribution of
\left(\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{z}_{i}/\sqrt{d}\right)_{i\leq M}

for
\boldsymbol{D}_{t}={\rm diag}\left(r_{1,t},...,r_{d,t}\right)
.
By Theorem
7
and Proposition
19
,
we have that for any
\delta>0
,

\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|{\cal R}\left(\nu_{M}^{t}\right)-{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)\right|\geq\delta\right\}\leq Ce^{-M}.

We claim that for all
t\geq 0
,

\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)\right\}-{\cal R}_{*}^{t}\right|\leq C\frac{\sqrt{d}}{M},

and for
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left\{{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)\right\}\right|\geq\delta\right\}\leq C\exp\left(-C\delta^{1/6}\left(1+\sqrt{d/M}\right)^{-1/6}M^{1/12}\right).

Using these claims, we then obtain for
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)

and all
t\geq 0
,

\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|{\cal R}\left(\nu_{M}^{t}\right)-{\cal R}_{*}^{t}\right|\geq\delta+C\frac{\sqrt{d}}{M}\right\}\leq C\exp\left(-C\delta^{1/6}\left(1+\sqrt{d/M}\right)^{-1/6}M^{1/12}\right).

Hence we are left with verifying the claims. Before we proceed, let

\boldsymbol{Z}=\left(\boldsymbol{z}_{1},...,\boldsymbol{z}_{M}\right)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times d}
.
Then:

\displaystyle{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{x}-\frac{1}{M}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}-\frac{1}{M}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}-\frac{1}{M}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right)\right\rangle\right\}+\frac{1}{2M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{2d}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}-A_{1}+\frac{1}{2}A_{2},

for
\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}/d\right)

and
\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}={\rm diag}\left(\Sigma_{1},...,\Sigma_{d}\right)
.
We recall that
\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{2}\leq C

since
r_{i,t}\leq C
for any
i\in\left[d\right]
and
t\geq 0
.

Step 1 - Calculation of
\mathbb{E}\left\{{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)\right\}
.

We compute
\mathbb{E}\left\{{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)\right\}
.
By Stein’s lemma, we have:

\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{1}\right\}=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left\{\frac{1}{M}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right)\right\}\right\rangle\right\}=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\right\}=\frac{1}{2d}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}.

Next, notice that for a fixed
\boldsymbol{u}
and
\boldsymbol{a}=\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{M}\right)
,

\left(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{Z}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\left(\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}^{\perp}+\frac{\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\right),

where
\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times d}
comprises
of i.i.d.
\mathsf{N}\left(0,1\right)
entries independent of
\boldsymbol{a}
.
We apply this observation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\left({\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)+\frac{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\rangle\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\right\}+\frac{1}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{a}}\left\{\frac{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{4}}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv A_{2,1}+A_{2,2},

where step
\left(a\right)
is because
\mathbb{E}\left\{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\}=0
.
To compute
A_{2,2}
, recall that
\boldsymbol{a}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{M}\right)

and that
\sigma
is homogenous:

\displaystyle A_{2,2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{M}\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{g^{2}\sigma\left(g\right)^{2}\right\}+\frac{M\left(M-1\right)}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{g\sigma\left(g\right)\right\}^{2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{4d}+\frac{5}{Md}\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{4d}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}+O\left(\frac{1}{M}\right).

To compute
A_{2,1}
, let
\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}
be the
i
-th
row of
\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}
and
a_{i}
be the
i
-th entry
of
\boldsymbol{a}
:

\displaystyle A_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\left(\boldsymbol{I}_{d}-{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\right)\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{a}}\left\{\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)^{2}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}-2\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\rangle+\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv A_{2,1,1}+A_{2,1,2}+A_{2,1,3}.

We compute
A_{2,1,1}
:

A_{2,1,1}=\frac{1}{2M}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}^{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}=\frac{1}{2dM}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}.

We give a bound on
A_{2,1,2}
:

\displaystyle\left|A_{2,1,2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm op}}^{4}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\right\}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\right\}d}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}=\frac{C}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sqrt{d}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\leq C\frac{\sqrt{d}}{M}.

Likewise, we obtain a bound on
A_{2,1,3}
:

\displaystyle\left|A_{2,1,3}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm op}}^{4}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\right\}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}=\frac{C}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\leq\frac{C}{M}.

Combining these calculations, we obtain an estimate on
\mathbb{E}\left\{{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)\right\}
:

\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)\right\}-{\cal R}_{*}^{t}\right|\leq C\frac{\sqrt{d}}{M},

since, recalling the definition of
{\cal R}_{*}^{t}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{d}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4d}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2dM}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Sigma_{i}^{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{2}r_{i,t}^{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2dM}\sum_{i=1}^{d}r_{i,t}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}r_{i,t}^{2}\Sigma_{i}^{2}=2{\cal R}_{*}^{t}.

Step 2 - Concentration.

We show that
{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)
concentrates
around
\mathbb{E}\left\{{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)\right\}
.
We first consider
A_{1}
:

A_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{1}\right\}=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{i=1}^{M}X_{1,i}-\mathbb{E}\left\{X_{1,i}\right\},

in which

X_{1,i}=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{z}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\right)\right\rangle\right\}=\frac{1}{d}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{z}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left\{g\sigma\left(g\right)\right\}=\frac{1}{2d}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{z}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

For any positive integer
p
,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|X_{1,i}\right|^{p}\right\}=\frac{C^{p}}{d^{p}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{z}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq\frac{C^{p}}{d^{p}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{z}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}p^{p}.

This implies that
X_{1,i}
is
C
-sub-exponential, and hence, by
Lemma
34
, for
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|A_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{1}\right\}\right|\geq\delta\right\}\leq Ce^{-C\delta^{2}M},

which shows concentration for
A_{1}
.

Next we consider concentration of
A_{2}
. To do so, we bound its
“central”
p
-moment, for an even number
p
, recalling the
random variables
\boldsymbol{a}
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}
as
defined in the previous step and applying Jensen’s inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|A_{2}-\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}-\frac{1}{2M}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{Z}}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{M^{2}}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2M}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{M^{2}}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)+\frac{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\rangle\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2M}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C^{p}\left(A_{2,1,p}+A_{2,2,p}+A_{2,3,p}+\sqrt{A_{2,2,p}A_{2,4,p}}+\sqrt{A_{2,2,p}A_{2,5,p}}+A_{2,6,p}\right),

in which we define:

\displaystyle A_{2,1,p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{M}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|^{p}\right\},

\displaystyle A_{2,2,p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\},

\displaystyle A_{2,3,p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\left\|\frac{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\rangle\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{M^{2}}{4}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|^{p}\right\},

\displaystyle A_{2,4,p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\},

\displaystyle A_{2,5,p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\frac{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\rangle\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\},

\displaystyle A_{2,6,p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right),\frac{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\rangle\right\rangle\right|^{p}\right\}.

Here without loss of generality, we have defined
\left(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)

on a joint space such that
\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}
is independent
of
\boldsymbol{u}
and
\boldsymbol{a}
, and
\boldsymbol{a}|\boldsymbol{u}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{M}\right)
.
For convenience, we shall also take
\boldsymbol{a}=\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\boldsymbol{g}

for some
\boldsymbol{g}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{I}_{M}\right)
,
defined on the same joint space, independent of
\boldsymbol{u}

and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}
. Below we shall let the
\left(i,j\right)
-th
entry and
i
-th row of
\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}
be
\tilde{z}_{i,j}

and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}
respectively, the
i
-th entry
of
\boldsymbol{a}
(respectively,
\boldsymbol{u}
and
\boldsymbol{g}
)
be
a_{i}
(respectively,
u_{i}
and
g_{i}
). We also note and
recall a few useful bounds:

•

\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq\max_{i\in\left[d\right]}r_{i,t}\leq C
.

•

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq C^{p}d^{-p}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\sqrt{d}\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)
,
since
\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C

and
\left\|\sqrt{d}\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}

is a
\chi^{2}
random variable with degree of freedom
d
and thus
has its
p
-moment bounded by
C^{p}\left(d^{p}+p^{p}\right)
.

•

\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\left(d^{p}+p^{p}\right)

and
\mathbb{E}\left\{\sigma\left(g\right)^{2p}\right\}\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{g^{2p}\right\}\leq C^{p}p^{p}

for the same reason.

•

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[g\sigma\left(g\right)\right]^{2p}\right\}\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{g^{4p}\right\}\leq C^{p}p^{2p}

by the above.

We proceed with several steps.

Step 2.1 - Bounding
A_{2,1,p}
.

We have:

\displaystyle A_{2,1,p}

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\sigma\left(a_{i}\right),\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{j}\sigma\left(a_{j}\right)\right\rangle-\frac{M}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left[\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)^{2}\right]\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left[\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i\neq j}\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\sigma\left(a_{i}\right),\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{j}\sigma\left(a_{j}\right)\right\rangle\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv B_{1.1}+B_{1.2}+B_{1.3}.

We then bound
B_{1.1}
,
B_{1.2}
and
B_{1.3}
:

•

To bound
B_{1.1}
, we rewrite:

B_{1.1}=\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k,t}^{2}\left(\tilde{z}_{i,k}^{2}-1\right)\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)^{2}\right|^{p}\right\}.

Notice that
\left(r_{k,t}^{2}\left(\tilde{z}_{i,k}^{2}-1\right)\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)^{2}\right)_{i\leq M,\;k\leq d}

are independent conditional on
\boldsymbol{a}
and
\boldsymbol{u}
.
We also have
\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{r_{k,t}^{2}\left(\tilde{z}_{i,k}^{2}-1\right)\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)^{2}\middle|\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{u}\right\}=0
,
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|r_{k,t}^{2}\left(\tilde{z}_{i,k}^{2}-1\right)\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)^{2}\right|^{p}\right\}=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{r_{k,t}^{2p}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2p}\left|\tilde{z}_{i,k}^{2}-1\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\tilde{z}_{i,k}^{2}-1\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C^{p}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)p^{p}\left(p^{p}+1\right).

By Lemma
37
,

B_{1.1}\leq C^{p}p^{4p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{M^{3/2}}\right)^{p}.

•

To bound
B_{1.2}
, notice that
\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)_{i\leq M}

are independent conditional on
\boldsymbol{u}
,
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{a}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\middle|\boldsymbol{u}\right\}=0
,
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a}}\left\{\left|\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}d^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a}}\left\{\left|\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=C^{p}d^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right|^{p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq C^{p}d^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2p}+1\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C^{p}d^{p}\left(p^{p}+1\right)\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right).

By Lemma
37
,

B_{1.2}\leq C^{p}p^{3p}\left(\frac{d}{M^{3/2}}\right)^{p}.

•

To bound
B_{1.3}
, let
\boldsymbol{B}_{1.3,i}=\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\sigma\left(a_{i}\right)
.
For any
k\leq d
and
i\neq j
,

\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\tilde{z}_{ik}\tilde{z}_{jk}\right|^{p}\right\}=\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\tilde{z}_{ik}\right|^{p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\tilde{z}_{jk}\right|^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}p^{p}.

So Lemma
37
implies

\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{j}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}p^{2p}d^{p/2}.

As such, we obtain for any
i\neq j
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{B}_{1.3,i},\boldsymbol{B}_{1.3,j}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\left|\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{j}\right\rangle\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\sigma\left(g_{j}\right)\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{j}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|g_{i}\right|^{p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|g_{j}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)p^{3p}d^{p/2}.

To proceed, we follow an argument similar to the proof of Lemma
37
.
We observe that
\left|\sum_{i\neq j}\left\langle\boldsymbol{B}_{1.3,i},\boldsymbol{B}_{1.3,j}\right\rangle\right|^{p}

is a sum of terms of the form
H=\prod_{k=1}^{p}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{k},\boldsymbol{b}_{2k}\right\rangle
,
where
\boldsymbol{b}_{k}\in\left\{\boldsymbol{B}_{1.3,i}\right\}_{i\leq M}

for
k=1,...,2p
such that
\boldsymbol{b}_{k}\neq\boldsymbol{b}_{2k}
.
Suppose
H
has
q_{i}
repeats of
\boldsymbol{B}_{1.3,i}
, where

\sum_{i=1}^{M}q_{i}=2p
. By Holder’s inequality and the bound on

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{B}_{1.3,i},\boldsymbol{B}_{1.3,j}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\right\}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|H\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{k=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{k},\boldsymbol{b}_{2k}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\right\}^{q_{i}/\left(2p\right)}\leq\prod_{k=1}^{p}\left[C^{p}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)p^{3p}d^{p/2}\right]^{q_{i}/\left(2p\right)}

\displaystyle=C^{p}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)p^{3p}d^{p/2}.

Observe that
\mathbb{E}\left\{H\right\}=0
if there exists some

i\in\left[M\right]
such that
q_{i}
is odd since
\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}

is symmetric. As proven in the proof of Lemma
37
,
the number of terms
H
such that no
q_{i}
is odd is upper-bounded
by
\left(2p\right)!M^{p}\leq 4^{p}p^{2p}M^{p}
. Hence

B_{1.3}=\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\sum_{i\neq j}\left\langle\boldsymbol{B}_{1.3,i},\boldsymbol{B}_{1.3,j}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)p^{5p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{M}\right)^{p}.

These bounds yield

A_{2,1,p}\leq C^{p}p^{6p}\left(1+\frac{d}{M}\right)^{p}\frac{1}{M^{p/2}}.

Step 2.2 - Bounding
A_{2,2,p}
.

We bound
A_{2,2,p}
:

\displaystyle A_{2,2,p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\frac{\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\langle\frac{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}},\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\rangle^{2p}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right|^{2p}\right\}=\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k,t}\tilde{z}_{i,k}u_{k}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right|^{2p}\right\}.

We have
\left(r_{k,t}\tilde{z}_{i,k}u_{k}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right)_{i\leq M,\;k\leq d}

are independent conditional on
\boldsymbol{u}
and
\boldsymbol{g}
.
Furthermore we also have
\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{r_{k,t}\tilde{z}_{i,k}u_{k}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\middle|\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g}\right\}=0

and, by recalling
\boldsymbol{u}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}/d\right)

with
\Sigma_{k}\leq C
for all
k\in\left[d\right]
,

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|r_{k,t}\tilde{z}_{i,k}u_{k}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right|^{2p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\tilde{z}_{i,k}^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{u_{k}^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{g_{i}^{2p}\right\}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{3p}}{d^{p}}.

Then by applying Lemma
37
, we obtain:

A_{2,2,p}\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\frac{p^{5p}}{d^{p}}\left(Md\right)^{p}=\frac{C^{p}}{M^{p}}p^{5p}.

Step 2.3 - Bounding
A_{2,3,p}
.

Note that

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right).

We then have a bound on
A_{2,3,p}
:

\displaystyle A_{2,3,p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{g},\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{g}\right)\right\rangle^{2}-\frac{M^{2}}{4}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}g_{i}^{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2}+\sum_{i\neq j\leq M}g_{i}g_{j}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\sigma\left(g_{j}\right)-\frac{M^{2}}{4}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}g_{i}^{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2}+\sum_{i\neq j\leq M}g_{i}g_{j}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\sigma\left(g_{j}\right)-\frac{M^{2}}{4}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left(g_{i}^{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2}-1.5\right)\right|^{p}\right\}+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|\frac{M-1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left(g_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)-0.5\right)\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sum_{j\leq M,\;j\neq i}g_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\left(g_{j}\sigma\left(g_{j}\right)-0.5\right)\right|^{p}\right\}+M^{p}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv C^{p}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)\left(B_{3.1}+B_{3.2}+B_{3.3}+M^{-p}\right).

We bound each term:

•

To bound
B_{3.1}
, notice that
\left(g_{i}^{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2}-1.5\right)_{i\leq M}

are independent,
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{g_{i}^{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2}-1.5\right\}=0

and

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|g_{i}^{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2}-1.5\right|^{p}\right\}\leq\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{g_{i}^{2p}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2p}+1.5^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}p^{2p}.

By Lemma
37
,

B_{3.1}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{3p}}{M^{1.5p}}.

•

To bound
B_{3.2}
, notice that
\left(g_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)-0.5\right)_{i\leq M}

are independent,
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{g_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)-0.5\right\}=0
,
and

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|g_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)-0.5\right|^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|g_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right|^{p}\right\}+1\right)\leq C^{p}p^{p}.

By Lemma
37
,

B_{3.2}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{2p}}{M^{p/2}}.

•

To bound
B_{3.3}
, notice that for a fixed
i
,
\left(g_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\left(g_{j}\sigma\left(g_{j}\right)-0.5\right)\right)_{j\leq M,\;j\neq i}

are independent conditional on
g_{i}
,
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{g_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\left(g_{j}\sigma\left(g_{j}\right)-0.5\right)\middle|g_{i}\right\}=0

and

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|g_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\left(g_{j}\sigma\left(g_{j}\right)-0.5\right)\right|^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|g_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right|^{p}\right\}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|g_{j}\sigma\left(g_{j}\right)\right|^{p}\right\}+1\right)\leq C^{p}p^{2p}.

By Lemma
37
,

B_{3.3}\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|\sum_{j\leq M,\;j\neq i}g_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\left(g_{j}\sigma\left(g_{j}\right)-0.5\right)\right|^{p}\right\}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{3p}}{M^{p/2-1}}.

We thus obtain:

A_{2,3,p}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{4p}}{M^{p/2-1}}.

Step 2.4 - Bounding
A_{2,4,p}
.

We bound
A_{2,4,p}
:

\displaystyle A_{2,4,p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{a}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}=\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}.

Notice that
\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right)_{i\leq M}

are independent,
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right\}=\boldsymbol{0}
,
and

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}=\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\left(d^{p}+p^{p}\right)p^{p},

which yields, by Lemma
37
,

A_{2,4,p}\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{p}}\left(d^{p}+p^{p}\right)p^{4p}.

Step 2.5 - Bounding
A_{2,5,p}
.

We have:

\displaystyle A_{2,5,p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\left\langle\boldsymbol{g},\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{g}\right)\right\rangle^{2p}\right\}\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{g}\right\|_{2}^{4p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)\left(M^{2p}+p^{2p}\right)\leq C^{p}p^{3p}.

Step 2.6 - Bounding
A_{2,6,p}
.

We have:

\displaystyle A_{2,6,p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{g},\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{g}\right)\right\rangle\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{M}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2}g_{i}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C^{p}}{M^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\sum_{j\neq i,\;j\leq M}\left(g_{j}\sigma\left(g_{j}\right)-0.5\right)\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv B_{6.1}+B_{6.2}+B_{6.3}.

We bound each of the terms:

•

To bound
B_{6.1}
, we have for a fixed
i
,
\left(\tilde{z}_{ij}u_{j}\right)_{j\leq d}

are independent,
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\tilde{z}_{ij}u_{j}\right\}=0

and
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sqrt{d}\tilde{z}_{ij}u_{j}\right|^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}p^{p}
.
We thus get from Lemma
37
:

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\right\}=d^{p/2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sqrt{d}\tilde{z}_{ij}u_{j}\right|^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}p^{2p}.

Observe that
\left(\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right)_{i\leq M}

are independent conditional on
\boldsymbol{u}
. We also have
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\middle|\boldsymbol{u}\right\}=0

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\right|^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle=C^{p}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{\left|g\right|^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\sqrt{\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)^{2}p^{p}}p^{p/2}\leq C^{p}p^{2p}.

Then by Lemma
37
,

B_{6.1}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{3p}}{M^{p/2}}.

•

We bound
B_{6.2}
:

\displaystyle B_{6.2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)^{2}g_{i}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\right|^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|g_{i}\right|^{3p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{p-1}}\left(1+\left(p/d\right)^{p}\right)p^{2p}\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{p-1}}p^{3p}.

•

To bound
B_{6.3}
, let
B_{6.3,i,j}=\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\sigma\left(g_{i}\right)\left(g_{j}\sigma\left(g_{j}\right)-0.5\right)
.
We have, for a fixed
i
,
\left(B_{6.3,i,j}\right)_{j\neq i,\;j\leq M}

are independent conditional on
\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}
,
\boldsymbol{u}

and
g_{i}
, and
\mathbb{E}\left\{B_{6.3,i,j}\middle|\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{u},g_{i}\right\}=0
.
In addition,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|B_{6.3,i,j}\right|^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\right|^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|g_{i}\right|^{p}\right\}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left|g_{j}\right|^{2p}\right\}+1\right)\leq C^{p}p^{3p}.

Then by Lemma
37
,

B_{6.3}\leq\frac{C^{p}}{M^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{g},\tilde{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left\{\left|\sum_{j\neq i,\;j\leq M}B_{6.3,i,j}\right|^{p}\right\}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{4p}}{M^{p/2-1}}.

Combining the bounds, recalling
p
is even, we thus get:

A_{2,6,p}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{4p}}{M^{p/2-1}}.

Step 2.7 - Finishing the concentration of
{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)
.

Collecting all the bounds in the previous steps, we then obtain:

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|A_{2}-\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}-\frac{1}{2M}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\right|^{p}\right\}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{6p}\left(1+d/M\right)^{p}}{M^{p/2-1}}.

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4d}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2dM}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{D}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{M}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}+\frac{1}{2M}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{M}\right).

We thus get

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|A_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}\right|^{p}\right\}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{6p}\left(1+d/M\right)^{p}}{M^{p/2-1}}.

This bound applies to even
p
and consequently odd
p
, since for
odd
p
:

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|A_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}\right|^{p}\right\}\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|A_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}\right|^{p+1}\right\}^{p/\left(p+1\right)}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{6p}\left(1+d/M\right)^{p}}{M^{p/2-p/\left(p+1\right)}}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{6p}\left(1+d/M\right)^{p}}{M^{p/2-1}}.

With the same argument, for an arbitrary integer
m\geq 1
, we have
for any
p\leq m
,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|A_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}\right|^{p}\right\}\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|A_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}\right|^{m}\right\}^{p/m}\leq\frac{C^{p}p^{6p}\left(1+d/M\right)^{p}}{M^{p/2-p/m}},

and therefore,

\max_{p\leq m,\;p\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}}\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|A_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}\right|^{p}\right\}^{1/\left(6p\right)}\leq\max_{p\leq m,\;p\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}}\frac{C\left(1+d/M\right)^{1/6}}{M^{1/12-1/\left(6mp\right)}}=\frac{C\left(1+d/M\right)^{1/6}}{M^{1/12-1/\left(6m\right)}}.

We also have:

\sup_{p\geq m}\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|A_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}\right|^{p}\right\}^{1/\left(6p\right)}\leq\sup_{p\geq m}\frac{C\left(1+d/M\right)^{1/6}}{M^{1/12-1/\left(6p\right)}}\leq\frac{C\left(1+d/M\right)^{1/6}}{M^{1/12-1/\left(6m\right)}}.

Therefore,

\sup_{p\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}}\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|A_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}\right|^{p}\right\}^{1/\left(6p\right)}\leq\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{C\left(1+d/M\right)^{1/6}}{M^{1/12-1/\left(6m\right)}}=\frac{C\left(1+d/M\right)^{1/6}}{M^{1/12}}.

Hence
\left|A_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}\right|^{1/6}

is sub-exponential with
\left\|\left|A_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}\right|^{1/6}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\left(1+d/M\right)^{1/6}M^{-1/12}
,
which yields the following concentration bound by Lemma
34
:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|A_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\{A_{2}\right\}\right|\geq\delta\right\}\leq C\exp\left(-C\delta^{1/6}\left(1+d/M\right)^{-1/6}M^{1/12}\right).

for any
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
. Combining with the concentration
of
A_{1}
, we get:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left\{{\cal R}\left(\bar{\nu}_{M}^{t}\right)\right\}\right|\geq\delta\right\}\leq C\exp\left(-C\delta^{1/6}\left(1+d/M\right)^{-1/6}M^{1/12}\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

4.4
Setting with ReLU activation: Proofs of auxiliary results

Proposition 16
.

Consider setting
[S.1
.
The following hold:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}\right)-\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1};\rho\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2};\rho\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2},

where
\rho=\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1}^{2},...,r_{d}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}/d\right)

with
\max_{i\leq d}r_{i}^{2}\leq C
. Furthermore,
\left|V\left(\boldsymbol{0}\right)\right|=\left|U\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{0}\right)\right|=\left|W\left(\boldsymbol{0};\rho\right)\right|=0

for any
\rho
.

Proof.

With the given
\rho
, we have from Stein’s lemma:

\int\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\rho\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1}^{2},...,r_{d}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}.

This yields, again by Stein’s lemma,

\displaystyle W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right),\int\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\rho\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right),\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1}^{2},...,r_{d}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\rm diag}\left(r_{1}\Sigma_{1},...,r_{d}\Sigma_{d}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

One can also compute
V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
:

\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}=\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\rm diag}\left(\Sigma_{1},...,\Sigma_{d}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2},

which yields

V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)=-\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\rm diag}\left(\Sigma_{1},...,\Sigma_{d}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}=-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Therefore:

\displaystyle\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\theta}+2\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta},

\displaystyle\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1}^{2}\Sigma_{1}^{2},...,r_{d}^{2}\Sigma_{d}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}.

Since
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C
,
one easily deduces the claims on
\nabla V
and
\nabla_{1}W
.

Next we consider
U
:

\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\kappa^{2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}+\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\kappa^{3}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\right\}.

We give a bound on
\left\|\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{2}
.
For the first term:

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\kappa^{2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)^{2}\right\}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}g\right)^{2}\right\}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Denoting the second term
\boldsymbol{v}
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\left(\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\geq 0\right\}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)^{3}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right\}\right)^{1/3}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}g\right)^{3}\right\}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}g\right|^{3}\right\}\right)^{1/3}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2},

which then yields

\left\|\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Lastly, it is easy to see that
V\left(\boldsymbol{0}\right)=U\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{0}\right)=W\left(\boldsymbol{0};\rho\right)=0

for any
\rho
.
∎

Proposition 17
.

Consider setting
[S.1
.
Then:

\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{1}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{2}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\max_{i\in\left[d\right]}\left|r_{i,1}-r_{i,2}\right|

where
\rho_{j}=\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1,j}^{2},...,r_{d,j}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}/d\right)
,

j=1,2
, with
\max_{i\leq d,\;j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}r_{i,j}^{2}\leq C
.

Proof.

The claim follows easily from the following formula given in the proof
of Proposition
16
:

\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{j}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1,j}^{2}\Sigma_{1}^{2},...,r_{d,j}^{2}\Sigma_{d}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta},\qquad j=1,2,

along with the fact
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C
.
∎

Proposition 18
.

Consider setting
[S.1
.
We have:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{121}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}},\left\|\nabla_{122}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{12}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2},

for any
\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Proof.

We have:

\displaystyle\nabla_{12}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}\boldsymbol{I}_{d}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime\top}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\},

\displaystyle\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime\top}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}.

Therefore, for
\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{c}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{121}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right],\boldsymbol{a}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}\otimes\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{5}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3}+A_{4}+A_{5}+A_{6},

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{122}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right],\boldsymbol{a}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}\otimes\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{5}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv B_{1}+B_{2}+B_{3}+B_{4}+B_{5}+B_{6},

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\nabla_{12}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{b}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv F_{1}+F_{2}+F_{3}+F_{4},

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{b}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle+\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}
