\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv H_{1}+H_{2}.

Let us consider
A_{1}
:

\displaystyle\left|A_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right|^{3}\right\}^{1/3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)^{3}\right\}^{1/3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right\}^{1/3}\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2}.

One can perform similar calculations to obtain:

\displaystyle\left|A_{1}\right|,\left|A_{2}\right|,\left|A_{4}\right|,\left|A_{5}\right|,\left|B_{1}\right|,\left|B_{3}\right|,\left|B_{4}\right|,\left|B_{5}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left|F_{1}\right|,\left|F_{2}\right|,\left|F_{3}\right|,\left|F_{4}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left|H_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2},

for a suitable constant
C
. We are left with
A_{3}
,
A_{6}
,

B_{2}
,
B_{6}
and
H_{2}
. Consider
A_{3}
:

A_{3}=\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right\},

for
\boldsymbol{z}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)
.
Notice that for
w=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)
,

\left(w,\boldsymbol{z}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\left(w,{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right),

for
\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)

independent of
w
. Therefore, using the fact
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\cdot\right)=\delta\left(\cdot\right)
,
it is easy to see that:

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{c},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right\},

in which we let
\boldsymbol{S}={\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}

for brevity. Since
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}\geq\kappa_{*}\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{S}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C
,
we have:

\left|A_{3}\right|\leq C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2}.

Similar calculations yield:

\displaystyle\left|A_{3}\right|,\left|A_{6}\right|,\left|B_{2}\right|,\left|B_{6}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left|H_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{121}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}},\left\|\nabla_{122}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{12}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2},

as claimed.
∎

Proposition 19
.

Consider setting
[S.1
.
Suppose that the initialization
\rho^{0}=\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},r_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{d}/d\right)

for
r_{0}\geq 0
. Then the ODE (
9
) admits as solution

\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t},\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
with

\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}=\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\frac{r_{1,t}}{r_{0}},...,\frac{r_{d,t}}{r_{0}}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{0},\qquad\rho^{t}=\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1,t}^{2},...,r_{d,t}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}/d\right),

in which
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{0}\sim\rho^{0}
and for each

i\in\left[d\right]
,

r_{i,t}=\sqrt{\frac{\Sigma_{i}^{2}-2\lambda}{0.5r_{0}^{2}\Sigma_{i}^{2}-\left(0.5r_{0}^{2}\Sigma_{i}^{2}-\Sigma_{i}^{2}+2\lambda\right)\exp\left\{-2\left(\Sigma_{i}^{2}-2\lambda\right)t\right\}}}r_{0}.

Here we take as a convention that if
r_{i,0}=0
then
r_{i,t}=0

and
r_{i,t}/r_{i,0}=1
. In fact,
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

is the unique weak solution, and under
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
,

\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
is the unique
solution to (
9
).

Proof.

We decompose the proof into two steps.

Verification of the proposed solution and trajectorial uniqueness.

It is easy to see that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}
admits
\rho^{t}

as the marginal and hence the claimed solution is consistent. We show
that
\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
is the
unique solution to the ODE under
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
,
which also shows
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
is a solution.
As calculated in the proof of Proposition
16
:

\displaystyle W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\rm diag}\left(r_{1,t}\Sigma_{1},...,r_{d,t}\Sigma_{d}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\rm diag}\left(\Sigma_{1},...,\Sigma_{d}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Then for any process
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

that satisfies the ODE (
9
) under
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
,

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}=-\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\alpha_{1,t},...,\alpha_{d,t}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t},\qquad\alpha_{i,t}=-\Sigma_{i}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}r_{i,t}^{2}\Sigma_{i}^{2}+2\lambda,

or equivalently,

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\left(\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}\right)=-{\rm diag}\left(\alpha_{1,t},...,\alpha_{d,t}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}\right).

Noticing that
r_{i,t}\geq 0
obeys the following differential equation
with initialization
r_{i,0}
:

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}r_{i,t}=-r_{i,t}\left(-\Sigma_{i}^{2}+2\lambda+\frac{1}{2}r_{i,t}^{2}\Sigma_{i}^{2}\right),

we have:

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\left(\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)={\rm diag}\left(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}r_{1,t},...,\frac{1}{r_{0}}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}r_{d,t}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{0}=-{\rm diag}\left(\alpha_{1,t},...,\alpha_{d,t}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}.

Hence
\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
is a
solution. We now show that it is the only solution. It suffices to
show that for each
i\in\left[d\right]
, the solution to the ODE

\left({\rm d}/{\rm d}t\right)u_{t}=-\alpha_{i,t}u_{t}
is unique.
Note that
r_{i,t}\leq\max\left\{r_{0},\sqrt{2\max\left(1-2\lambda/\Sigma_{i}^{2},0\right)}\right\}

and hence
\left|\alpha_{i,t}\right|\leq c
a constant for all
t\geq 0
.
Let
u_{1,t}
and
u_{2,t}
be two solutions with
u_{1,0}=u_{2,0}
.
We have:

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\left(\left(u_{1,t}-u_{2,t}\right)^{2}\right)=-2\alpha_{i,t}\left(u_{1,t}-u_{2,t}\right)^{2}\leq 2c\left(u_{1,t}-u_{2,t}\right)^{2}.

Since
u_{1,0}=u_{2,0}
, Gronwall’s lemma then implies that
u_{1,t}=u_{2,t}
,
and hence the solution must be unique.

Uniqueness in law.

We are left with proving that
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
is
the unique weak solution with the initialization
\rho^{0}
. To that
end, we take a detour here. Let
\left(\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

and
\left(\bar{\rho}_{2}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
be two solutions with
the same initialization
\bar{\rho}_{1}^{0}=\bar{\rho}_{2}^{0}=\bar{\rho}

(with the equalities holding in the weak sense) for a generic
\bar{\rho}\in\mathscr{P}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)

with finite second moment
B_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\equiv\int\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}\bar{\rho}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)<\infty
.
We define accordingly two coupled trajectories
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

and
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
with the
same initialization
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{0}=\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{0}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{0}\sim\bar{\rho}
:

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}

\displaystyle=-\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t};\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\right),\qquad\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}={\rm Law}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}

\displaystyle=-\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t};\bar{\rho}_{2}^{t}\right),\qquad\bar{\rho}_{2}^{t}={\rm Law}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right).

In the following, we let
c
be generic positive constants that may
differ at different instances of use and may depend on the dimension
vector
\mathfrak{Dim}
, but not the time
t
or the initialization

\bar{\rho}
. We first obtain an a priori bound on
B_{1,t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}
.
By Proposition
16
,

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t};\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}+\int\left\|\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right\|_{2}\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}+c\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}\int\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

from which we obtain

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}B_{1,t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\leq c\left(1+B_{1,t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right)B_{1,t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\leq c\left(1+eB_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right)B_{1,t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right),

for
t<t_{*}=\inf\left\{t\geq 0:\;B_{1,t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)>eB_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right\}
.
Gronwall’s lemma then yields:

B_{1,t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\leq B_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\exp\left\{c\left(1+eB_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right)t\right\},

which holds for
t<t_{*}
. Therefore, with
1/T=c\left(1+eB_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right)
,
we have
B_{1,t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\leq eB_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)

for all
t\leq T
. By the same procedure, we have the same result
for
B_{2,t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}
.
Next we bound the distance between the two trajectories:

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right)-\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t};\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t};\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t};\bar{\rho}_{2}^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t};\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}.

Define
M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}
.
By Propositions
16
and
18

and the mean value theorem, for
t\leq T
:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right)-\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t};\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t};\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int\left\|\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right\|_{2}\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{\leq}}\int\left\|\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}\int\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}B_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t};\bar{\rho}_{2}^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t};\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{=}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\right)-\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right)\right\}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\left\|\nabla_{12}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}\right\|_{2}\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}}+M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}\left(\sqrt{B_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)}+M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right),

where in step
\left(a\right)
,
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{1}\in\left[\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right]
;
in step
\left(b\right)
, we define
\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right)

and
\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\right)

is independent of
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right)
;
in step
\left(c\right)
,
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}\in\left[\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\right]

and hence
\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}
.
These bounds imply that

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq c\left(1+B_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+c\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}\left(\sqrt{B_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)}+M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right).

Taking expectation, we obtain:

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\leq c\left(1+B_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right)M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)+c\sqrt{B_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)}\left(\sqrt{B_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)}+M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right)\leq c\left(1+B_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\right)M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right),

for
t\leq T
and
t<t_{*}^{\prime}
with
t_{*}^{\prime}=\inf\left\{t\geq 0:\;M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)>1\right\}
.
Since
M_{0}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)=0
and
M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)\geq 0
,
Gronwall’s lemma then implies that
t_{*}^{\prime}>T
and
M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)=0

for
t\leq T
. Note that
M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)=0
implies,
for any
1
-Lipschitz test function
\phi:\;\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\left|\int\phi\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-\int\phi\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\bar{\rho}_{2}^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right|\leq\inf_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{a}\sim\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t},\;\boldsymbol{\theta}_{b}\sim\bar{\rho}_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{a}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{b}\right\|_{2}\right\}\leq\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}\right\}\leq\sqrt{M_{t}\left(\bar{\rho}\right)}=0.

Hence two solutions
\left(\bar{\rho}_{1}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
and

\left(\bar{\rho}_{2}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
coincide (weakly) up to
time
T
.

Applying this result to our problem, we suppose that, for a fixed

s\geq 0
, two solutions
\left(\rho_{1}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
and

\left(\rho_{2}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
coincide (weakly) with
\rho^{s}=\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(r_{1,s}^{2},...,r_{d,s}^{2}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}/d\right)

at time
t=s
. Then the above result shows that they coincide (weakly)
on the time interval
\left[s,s+T_{s}\right]
, in which

\frac{1}{T_{s}}=c\left(1+e\int\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}\rho^{s}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)=c\left(1+\frac{e}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}r_{i,s}^{2}\right)\leq c\left(1+e\left(r_{0}^{2}+2\right)\right),

using the observation
r_{i,s}\leq\max\left\{r_{0},\sqrt{2\max\left(1-2\lambda/\Sigma_{i}^{2},0\right)}\right\}\leq\max\left\{r_{0},\sqrt{2}\right\}\leq C

which holds for all
i\in\left[d\right]
and
s\geq 0
. Since
T_{s}

is lower-bounded by a strictly positive constant independent of
s\geq 0
,
the solution
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
must be the unique
weak solution on
t\in[0,\infty)
with initialization
\rho^{0}
.

∎

Proposition 20
.

Consider setting
[S.1
.
For a collection of vectors
\Theta=\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)_{i\leq N}

where
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\boldsymbol{x}\sim{\cal P}

and
\boldsymbol{z}=\left(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{x}\right)
,
we have
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)
is
sub-exponential with
\psi_{1}
-norm:

\left\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}+1\right).

Proof.

Consider a fixed vector
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
:

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\nabla_{2}\sigma_{*}\left(\boldsymbol{x};\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)^{\top}\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{y}}_{N}\left(\boldsymbol{x};\Theta\right)-\boldsymbol{x}\right)\right\rangle+\lambda\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\nabla_{1}\Lambda\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)+\kappa^{2}\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle+2\lambda\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3},

where we denote
\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}=\left(1/N\right)\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{N}\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)

for brevity. We examine each component in the above:

•

For any
i\in\left[N\right]
, since
\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\leq\left|\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right|
,

\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}
,
we have
\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)

is sub-Gaussian with
\psi_{2}
-norm
\left\|\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}
.
Therefore for any
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle

is sub-Gaussian with
\psi_{2}
-norm

\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\frac{\kappa}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\right\rangle\right|\left\|\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\kappa\mathsf{M}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2},

where
\mathsf{M}=\left(1/N\right)\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}
.
We have
\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle

is sub-Gaussian with
\psi_{2}
-norm
\left\|\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}=\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}
.
Therefore,
A_{1}
is sub-exponential:

\displaystyle\left\|A_{1}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\left\|\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\left(\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}+\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\left(\kappa\mathsf{M}+\frac{1}{\kappa}\right)\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\left(\mathsf{M}+1\right).

•

Recall that
\sigma^{\prime}\left(u\right)=\mathbb{I}\left(u\geq 0\right)

and hence
\left\|\sigma^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Then
A_{2}

is sub-exponential:

\displaystyle\left\|A_{2}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\left(\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}+\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\right)\left\|\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa\left(\kappa\mathsf{M}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\kappa}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\right)\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\left(\mathsf{M}+1\right).

•

A_{3}
is a constant and so it is also sub-exponential with
\psi_{1}
-norm

\left\|A_{3}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}
.

We have
\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\rangle

and hence
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)

are sub-exponential:

\left\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\|_{\psi_{1}}=\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\left(\mathsf{M}+1\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 21
.

Consider setting
[S.1
.
We have, for some sufficiently large
C_{*}
, with probability at
least
1-C\exp\left(Cd-CN\kappa_{*}^{2}/\kappa^{2}\right)
,

\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C_{*},

in which
\boldsymbol{\zeta}
is a fixed vector with
\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}<\infty
,

\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)_{i\leq N}\sim_{{\rm i.i.d.}}\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}/d\right)

and
\boldsymbol{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
with
\left\|\boldsymbol{D}\right\|_{2}\leq C
.
Here
C_{*}
does not depend on
d
or
N
.

Proof.

Let us decompose

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)=\boldsymbol{M}_{1}+\boldsymbol{M}_{1}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d},

for which

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}.

Below we bound
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
separately.
We shall use repeatedly the following simple fact:
\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right|^{m}\right\}=0.5

for any
m>0
, since
\sigma^{\prime}\left(u\right)=\mathbb{I}\left(u\geq 0\right)
.

Step 1: Bounding
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.

Define the quantity
A_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\kappa^{2}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}\right\|_{2}
.
Note that for any
\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\right|=\left|\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right|^{3}\right\}^{1/3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\kappa\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right\}^{1/3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\kappa\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right\}^{1/3}

\displaystyle\quad=C\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2},

and therefore
A_{1}\leq C
. Furthermore, we have:

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}-A_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}-\frac{1}{2}\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{D}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}\right]\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\equiv\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}.

Here we making the following claim:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\geq\delta\right\}\leq C\exp\left(Cd-C\delta^{2}N/\kappa^{2}\right),

for
\delta\geq 0
. Assuming this claim, we thus have for
\delta\geq 0

and some sufficiently large
C^{\prime}
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\geq C^{\prime}+\delta\right\}\leq C\exp\left(Cd-C\delta^{2}N/\kappa^{2}\right),

which is the desired result.

We are left with proving the claim on
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.
Given fixed
\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}},\qquad M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}=\kappa\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right\}.

First notice that
\left(M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right)_{i\leq N}

are i.i.d. Furthermore, by Stein’s lemma,

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}=\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}.

Therefore
\mathbb{E}\left\{M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right\}=0
.
For any positive integer
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right\}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)^{2}\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle^{2}\right\}^{p/2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle^{2}\right\}^{p/2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle^{2}\right\}^{p/2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle^{2}\right\}^{p/2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\kappa^{2}\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle^{2}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle^{2}\right\}^{p/2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle^{2}\right\}^{p/2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\kappa^{2}\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{p/2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\left\|\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{p}+1\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}}+1\right)

\displaystyle=C^{p}\left(\sqrt{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{g^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}}+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\left(\sqrt{p^{p}\left(\kappa^{2p}+p^{p}\right)}+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\left(\kappa^{p}p^{p/2}+p^{p}\right),

recalling that
\left\|\sigma^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 1
for

\sigma
being the ReLU,
\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)
,

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C
,
\left\|\boldsymbol{D}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}=1
.
Here we have used the fact that if
X
is a
\chi^{2}
random variable
with degree of freedom
\kappa^{2}
, then
\mathbb{E}\left\{X^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\left(\kappa^{2}+2p\right)^{p}
.
It is easy to see that
M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}

is a sub-exponential random variable with
\psi_{1}
-norm
\left\|M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\kappa
.
Then by Lemma
34
, for
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
,
with probability at most
C\exp\left(-C\delta^{2}N/\kappa^{2}\right)
,

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|=\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right|\geq\delta.

Now we construct an epsilon-net
{\cal N}\subset\mathbb{S}^{d-1}

such that for any
\boldsymbol{a}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, there exists

\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}\in{\cal N}
with
\left\|\boldsymbol{a}-\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\leq 1/3
.
There is such an epsilon-net
{\cal N}
with size
\left|{\cal N}\right|\leq 9^{d}

[
Ver10
]
. A standard argument yields

\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq 3\max_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\in{\cal N}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle.

Therefore, by the union bound, we obtain:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\geq\delta\right\}\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\in{\cal N}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\geq\delta/3\right\}\leq C\exp\left(Cd-C\delta^{2}N/\kappa^{2}\right).

This proves the claim.

Step 2: Bounding
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.

The procedure is similar to the bounding of
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
,
with some tweaks. In particular, for
\sigma
being the ReLU,
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\cdot\right)=\delta\left(\cdot\right)

the Dirac-delta function, which presents technical challenges that
we circumvent in the following. To lighten notations, define
\boldsymbol{Q}=\kappa\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},...,\boldsymbol{\theta}_{N}\right)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times d}
.
One can then rewrite:

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)=\frac{1}{N}\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\sigma\left(\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}\right).

We have:

\boldsymbol{M}_{2}=\kappa\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\frac{1}{N}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\sigma\left(\frac{1}{\kappa}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{z}\boldsymbol{z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\},

where
\boldsymbol{z}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)
.
Notice that for
w=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)
,

\left(w,\boldsymbol{z}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\left(w,{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right),

for
\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)

independent of
w
. Therefore, using the fact
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\cdot\right)=\delta\left(\cdot\right)
,
it is easy to see that:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\frac{1}{N}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\sigma\left(\frac{1}{\kappa}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\left({\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right)\right)\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\top}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\frac{1}{N}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\sigma\left(\frac{1}{\kappa}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\left({\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right)\right)\right\rangle\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\top}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\frac{1}{N}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\sigma\left(\frac{1}{\kappa}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\left({\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right)\right)\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\frac{1}{N}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\sigma\left(\frac{1}{\kappa}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\left({\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right)\right)\right\rangle\frac{w^{2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{4}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\frac{1}{N}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\sigma\left(\frac{1}{\kappa}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\right\rangle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}^{\top}\right\},

in which we let
\boldsymbol{S}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}

for brevity.

After this simplification, the analysis of
\boldsymbol{M}_{2}
is
similar to
\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}
. Given fixed
\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}},\qquad M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}=\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right\}.

First notice that
\left(M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right)_{i\leq N}

are i.i.d. By Stein’s lemma,

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)\right\}=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)\right\}\frac{1}{\kappa}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}.

This yields

\mathbb{E}\left\{M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right\}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2\pi}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right\}=0,

since
\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}
is symmetric. Next, for any positive
integer
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle=C^{p}\frac{\kappa^{p}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{p}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right\}^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\frac{\kappa^{p}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{p}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)^{3}\right\}^{p/3}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right\}^{p/3}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right\}^{p/3}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\frac{\kappa^{p}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{p}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\left\|\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{p}\left\|\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{p}\left\|\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\frac{1}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{p}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\left\|\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\frac{1}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{p}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\right|^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}}

\displaystyle=C^{p}\frac{\left\|\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{p}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{p}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{g^{2p}\right\}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\kappa_{*}^{-p}\sqrt{p^{p}\left(\kappa^{2p}+p^{p}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\kappa_{*}^{-p}\left(\kappa^{p}p^{p/2}+p^{p}\right)

following a reasoning similar to the bounding procedure of
\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}
,
where we note we have used
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}\geq C\kappa_{*}\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{D}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}
.
We conclude that
M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}
is a sub-exponential
random variable with
\psi_{1}
-norm
\left\|M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\kappa/\kappa_{*}
.
Therefore, by Lemma
34
, for
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
,
with probability at most
C\exp\left(-C\delta^{2}N\kappa_{*}^{2}/\kappa^{2}\right)
,

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|=\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right|\geq\delta.

Now we can reuse the same epsilon-net argument in the analysis of

\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}
to obtain:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\geq\delta\right\}\leq C\exp\left(Cd-C\delta^{2}N\kappa_{*}^{2}/\kappa^{2}\right).

Step 3: Putting all together.

From the bounds on
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\geq C_{*}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(Cd-C\delta^{2}N/\kappa^{2}\right)+C\exp\left(Cd-CN\kappa_{*}^{2}/\kappa^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(Cd-CN\kappa_{*}^{2}/\kappa^{2}\right),

for sufficiently large
C_{*}
, recalling
\kappa_{*}\leq C
and
choosing suitable
\delta\leq C\kappa_{*}
. This completes the proof.

∎

Proposition 22
.

Consider setting
[S.1
.
We have, for some sufficiently large
C_{*}
, with probability at
least
1-\exp\left(Cd\log\left(\kappa/\kappa_{*}+e\right)-CN\kappa_{*}^{2}/\kappa^{2}\right)
,

\sup_{\left\|\boldsymbol{r}\right\|_{\infty}\leq r_{*}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq r_{*}^{2}C_{*},

in which
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)_{i\leq N}\sim_{{\rm i.i.d.}}\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}/d\right)

and
r_{*}\geq 0
. Here
C_{*}
does not depend on
d
,
N
or

r_{*}
.

Proof.

The proof leverages on Lemma
21
and comprises
of several steps. First of all, we note that
\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}

since
\boldsymbol{R}
is orthogonal. Hence we can equivalently study
the quantity:

Q=\sup_{\left\|\boldsymbol{r}\right\|_{\infty}\leq r_{*}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}.

Step 1: Reduction of the supremization set.

First recall that

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)=\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)+\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)^{\top}+\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d},

for which

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}.

We make a few observations. Firstly, for any
c>0
, since
\sigma

is the ReLU,
\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\left(c\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)=\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)

and
\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},c\boldsymbol{r}\right)=c^{2}\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)
.
Secondly, as shown in the proof of Lemma
21
,

\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)=\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left\{\left\langle{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\frac{1}{N}\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\sigma\left(\frac{1}{\kappa}\boldsymbol{Q}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\rangle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{z}\boldsymbol{z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}^{\top}\right\},

for
\boldsymbol{z}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)

(see the proof of Lemma
21
for the definitions
of
\boldsymbol{Q}
and
\boldsymbol{S}
, which are unimportant
here). It is then easy to see that
\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\left(c\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)=\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)

and
\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},c\boldsymbol{r}\right)=c^{2}\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{r}\right)
.
Therefore, we obtain the following simplification:

Q=r_{*}^{2}\sup_{\left\|\boldsymbol{r}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in{\cal S}}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}

(24)

for
{\cal S}={\cal B}_{d}\left(1\right)\backslash{\cal B}_{d}\left(1/2\right)
.
Here the exclusion of
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=\boldsymbol{0}
from
{\cal S}

can be easily reasoned by a continuity argument.

Step 2: Epsilon-net argument.

From here onwards, we focus on the supremization over
\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in{\cal S}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{r}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Fix
\gamma\in\left(0,1/3\right)
.
Consider an epsilon-net
{\cal N}_{d}^{\infty}\left(\gamma\right)\subset\left\{\boldsymbol{r}:\;\left\|\boldsymbol{r}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 1\right\}

such that for any
\boldsymbol{r}
with
\left\|\boldsymbol{r}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 1
,
there exists
\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\in{\cal N}_{d}^{\infty}\left(\gamma\right)

with
\left\|\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma
.
Likewise, consider an epsilon-net
{\cal N}_{d}^{2}\left(\gamma\right)\subset{\cal S}

in which for any
\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in{\cal S}
, there exists
\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\in{\cal N}_{d}^{2}\left(\gamma\right)

such that
\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\leq\gamma
.
Note that
{\cal N}_{d}^{2}\left(\gamma\right)\subset{\cal B}_{d}\left(1\right)
.
A standard volumetric argument
[
Ver10
]
shows
that there exist such epsilon-nets with sizes

\left|{\cal N}_{d}^{\infty}\left(\gamma\right)\right|,\left|{\cal N}_{d}^{2}\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq\left(\frac{3}{\gamma}\right)^{d}.

Consider
\boldsymbol{r}
and
\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\in{\cal N}_{d}^{\infty}\left(\gamma\right)

such that
\left\|\boldsymbol{r}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and

\left\|\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma
,
and
\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in{\cal S}
and
\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\in{\cal N}_{d}^{2}\left(\gamma\right)

such that
\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\leq\gamma
.
We have from the mean value theorem:

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}-\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)-\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)-\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\nabla_{111}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\nabla_{121}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}}\left\|\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\nabla_{111}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}}\gamma+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\gamma

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\nabla_{111}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}}\gamma+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\gamma,

(25)

where in step
\left(a\right)
, we have
\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\in\left[\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\right]

and
\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\in\left[\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right]
;
in step
\left(b\right)
, we apply Proposition
18
;
in step
\left(c\right)
, we use the fact that

\left\|\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}+\gamma\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq 2\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}.

We note that since
\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\in\left[\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\right]
,

\left\|\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}\geq\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\geq 1/2-1/3=1/6.

(26)

To simplify the notations, let
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}=\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}
,
and note that
\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}
.
We have:

\nabla_{111}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right]=\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}+\boldsymbol{M}_{2,i}+\boldsymbol{M}_{3,i}+\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)^{\otimes 3},

for which

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\right\},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{2,i}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{3,i}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\right\},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{5}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\right\}.

Note that
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}=\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{2,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}=\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{3,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.
Then Eq. (
24
) and (
25
)
yield

\left|Q-Q_{\gamma}\right|\leq r_{*}^{2}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(3\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}+\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\right)\gamma+r_{*}^{2}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\gamma,

(27)

in which we define:

Q_{\gamma}=r_{*}^{2}\max_{\boldsymbol{r}\in{\cal N}_{d}^{\infty}\left(\gamma\right)}\max_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in{\cal N}_{d}^{2}\left(\gamma\right)}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{R}{\rm diag}\left(\boldsymbol{r}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}.

The next two steps are devoted to bounding
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.

Step 3: Bounding
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.

To bound
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
,
we have for any
\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{c}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i},\boldsymbol{a}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}\otimes\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right\},

where
\boldsymbol{z}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)
.
Notice that for
w_{i}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)
,

\left(w_{i},\boldsymbol{z}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\left(w_{i},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right),

in which
\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)

independent of
w_{i}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i},\boldsymbol{a}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}\otimes\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{w_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\Bigg\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w_{i}\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigg[\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle\Bigg]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{=}}\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{w_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\Bigg\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w_{i}\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{=}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{=}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{c},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(d\right)}}{{\leq}}C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)^{3}\right\}^{1/3}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right\}^{1/3}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{c},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right\}^{1/3}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(e\right)}}{{\leq}}C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2}.

where in steps
\left(a\right)
and
\left(b\right)
, we recall
that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\cdot\right)=\delta\left(\cdot\right)
the Dirac-delta
function and that
w_{i}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)
;
in step
\left(c\right)
, we have define
\boldsymbol{S}_{i}={\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}

for brevity; in step
\left(d\right)
, we use
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}\geq\kappa_{*}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}\geq 1/6
from Eq.
(
26
); in step
\left(e\right)
,
we use
\left\|\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C

and
\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}
.
Consequently we obtain:

\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Step 4: Bounding
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.

Owing to the presence of
\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}
for
\sigma
being the ReLU,
we need to treat the expectation in this term in the distributional
sense:

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(w\right)f\left(w\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{w}}\exp\left(-\frac{w^{2}}{2\sigma_{w}^{2}}\right){\rm d}w=-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}w}\left[f\left(w\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{w}}\exp\left(-\frac{w^{2}}{2\sigma_{w}^{2}}\right)\right]{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\qquad=-\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}w}\left[f\left(w\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{w}}\exp\left(-\frac{w^{2}}{2\sigma_{w}^{2}}\right)\right]_{w=0}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{w}}f^{\prime}\left(0\right).

In particular, reusing the same argument in the simplification of

\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}
, for
w_{i}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)
,
we have:

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i},\boldsymbol{a}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}\otimes\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{w_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\Bigg\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(w_{i}\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\times\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{b},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\Bigg\{\frac{\left\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{b},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{c},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{b},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{c},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle+\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{c},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{i}\boldsymbol{b},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\Bigg\},
