where we define
\boldsymbol{S}_{i}={\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}

for brevity. Then proceeding in a similar fashion to the bounding
of
\boldsymbol{M}_{1}
, one can easily show that

\left\langle\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i},\boldsymbol{a}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}\otimes\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle\leq C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}^{2}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2}.

In other words,

\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}^{2}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Step 5: Finishing the proof.

From the bounds on
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
and
Eq. (
27
) , we get:

\left|Q-Q_{\gamma}\right|\leq r_{*}^{2}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}C\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}^{2}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\gamma.

Notice that
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}

is a
\chi^{2}
random variable of degree of freedom
Nd=N\kappa^{2}
,
and therefore it is a standard concentration fact that for
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq N\kappa^{2}\left(1+\delta\right)\right\}\leq C\exp\left(-CN\kappa^{2}\delta^{2}\right).

Furthermore, using Lemma
21
and the union
bound, we obtain for sufficiently large
C_{*}
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{Q_{\gamma}\geq r_{*}^{2}C_{*}\right\}\leq\left|{\cal N}_{d}^{\infty}\left(\gamma\right)\right|\left|{\cal N}_{d}^{2}\left(\gamma\right)\right|C\exp\left(C\left(d-N\kappa_{*}^{2}/\kappa^{2}\right)\right)\leq\left(\frac{3}{\gamma}\right)^{2d}C\exp\left(C\left(d-N\kappa_{*}^{2}/\kappa^{2}\right)\right).

Let us choose
\gamma=\kappa_{*}^{2}/\left(C\kappa^{2}\right)<1/3

and
\delta=0.5
. Then for sufficiently large
C_{*}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{Q\geq r_{*}^{2}C_{*}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(-CN\kappa^{2}\right)+\left(\frac{C\kappa^{2}}{\kappa_{*}^{2}}\right)^{d}C\exp\left(Cd-CN\kappa_{*}^{2}/\kappa^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(-CN\kappa^{2}\right)+C\exp\left(Cd\log\left(\kappa/\kappa_{*}+e\right)-CN\kappa_{*}^{2}/\kappa^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(Cd\log\left(\kappa/\kappa_{*}+e\right)-CN\kappa_{*}^{2}/\kappa^{2}\right),

where we recall
\kappa_{*}\leq C
. This completes the proof.
∎

4.5
Setting with bounded activation: Proof of Theorem
15

We prove Theorem
15
. Our proof uses several
auxiliary results, which are stated and proven in Section
4.6
.

Proof of Theorem
15
.

The theorem follows from Propositions
25
,

26
,
28
,

29
,
30
,
31

and
33
. In particular, by Proposition

29
, the process
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t},\rho_{r}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

as described exists and is (weakly) unique. By Proposition
30
,
we have
\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t},\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

form the (weakly) unique solution to the ODE (
9
) with
initialization
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{0}\sim\rho^{0}
and
\rho^{0}

respectively, where

\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}=\left(r_{1,t}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}/\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}\right\|_{2},\quad r_{2,t}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}/\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}\right\|_{2}\right),\qquad\rho^{t}={\rm Law}\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right),

\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}/\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}\right\|_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}

and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}/\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}\right\|_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}

are independent of each other and of
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
.
We also have from Proposition
29
that
r_{1,t}

and
r_{2,t}
are
C
-sub-Gaussian for any
t\leq T
, and
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t}\right)

is a deterministic functions of their initialization
\left(r_{1,0},r_{2,0}\right)
,
i.e.
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t}\right)=\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,0},r_{2,0}\right)
,
such that
\left\|\partial_{t}\psi_{t}\left(r_{1},r_{2}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\left(1+t+r_{1}+r_{2}\right)
.
Using these facts and recalling the definition of the Wasserstein
distance
\mathscr{W}_{2}
in the statement of Proposition
26
,
we have for any
t_{1},t_{2}\leq T
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\rho_{r}^{t_{1}},\rho_{r}^{t_{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{r}\left\{\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left|\left(\psi_{t_{1}}\left(r_{1,0},r_{2,0}\right)\right)_{j}-\left(\psi_{t_{2}}\left(r_{1,0},r_{2,0}\right)\right)_{j}\right|^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{r}\left\{1+t_{1}^{2}+t_{2}^{2}+r_{1,0}^{2}+r_{2,0}^{2}\right\}\left|t_{2}-t_{1}\right|^{2}\leq C\left|t_{2}-t_{1}\right|^{2},

where we let
\mathbb{E}_{r}
denote the expectation over
\left(r_{1,0},r_{2,0}\right)
.
These verify Assumption
[A.1
and allow Propositions

25
,
26

and
33
to verify Assumptions
[A.3

and
[A.6
.

By Proposition
25
, for any
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\int\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\rho^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\int\left(\bar{r}_{1}q_{1}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}\bar{r}_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}},\quad\bar{r}_{2}q_{2}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}\bar{r}_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}}\right)\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right).

Note that for
\boldsymbol{x}\sim{\cal P}
,
\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\chi_{1}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\chi_{2}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle{\cal R}\left(\rho^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{x}-\int\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\rho^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left(\chi_{j}-\int\bar{r}_{j}q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}\bar{r}_{1},\chi_{2}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\right)^{2}\right\}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

4.6
Setting with bounded activation: Proofs of auxiliary results

Lemma 23
.

Consider
\boldsymbol{\omega}\sim\text{Unif}\left(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right)

and let
\omega_{1}
be its first entry, for
d>16
. Then

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\kappa\omega_{1}\right)^{8}\right\}\leq C,\qquad\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle^{8}\right\}\leq C,

for some constant
C
independent of
d
and any
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.

Proof.

We have, for
\left(g_{i}\right)_{i\leq d}\sim_{{\rm i.i.d.}}\mathsf{N}\left(0,1\right)
,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}^{2}\right)^{-8}\right\}=\frac{\Gamma\left(d/2-8\right)}{256\Gamma\left(d/2\right)}\leq\frac{1}{256}\left(\frac{d}{2}-8\right)^{-8}.

Note that
\omega_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}g_{1}/\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}^{2}}
.
By Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality, for
d>16
,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\kappa\omega_{1}\right)^{8}\right\}\leq d^{4}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\{g_{i}^{16}\right\}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}^{2}\right)^{-8}\right\}}\leq\frac{Cd^{4}}{\left(d/2-8\right)^{4}}\leq C,

uniformly in
d
. Next, for any
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,
by choosing an orthogonal
Q
such that
Q\boldsymbol{v}=\left(1,0,...,0\right)^{\top}
,
we get:

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle^{8}\right\}=\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\langle\kappa Q\boldsymbol{\omega},Q\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle^{8}\right\}=\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\omega},Q\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle^{8}\right\}=\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\kappa\omega_{1}\right)^{8}\right\}\leq C,

where we have used the fact
\boldsymbol{\omega}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}Q\boldsymbol{\omega}

for any orthogonal
Q
.
∎

Lemma 24
.

Consider
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
as
defined in (
21
) and (
22
).
The following quantities

\left|q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|\frac{1}{a}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|\frac{1}{b}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|\partial_{1}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|\partial_{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,

\left|\partial_{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|\partial_{1}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|b\partial_{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|a\partial_{1}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|a\partial_{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|b\partial_{1}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,

\left|a\partial_{1}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|b\partial_{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|\frac{a}{b}\partial_{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|\frac{b}{a}\partial_{1}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|\partial_{11}^{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|\partial_{22}^{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,

\left|a\partial_{11}^{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|b\partial_{22}^{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|a\partial_{22}^{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|b\partial_{11}^{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|a\partial_{12}^{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|,\;\left|b\partial_{12}^{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|,

are all bounded by some constant
C
independent of
\mathfrak{Dim}
,
for any
a,b\geq 0
, given that
d_{1},d_{2}>16
. (Here
\left|\left(1/a\right)\cdot f\left(a,b\right)\right|\leq C

should be interpreted as that
\left|f\left(a,b\right)\right|\leq Ca
,
which holds for any
a\geq 0
.)

Proof.

By Lemma
23
,
\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\kappa\omega_{11}\right)^{8}\right\},\;\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\kappa\omega_{21}\right)^{8}\right\}\leq C
.
We shall repeatedly use this fact, along with
\left\|\sigma\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\sigma^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\sigma^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C
,
without stating explicitly. We have
\left|q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|\leq\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\left|\kappa\omega_{11}\right|\right\}\leq C
.
One can perform similar arguments to deduce the bounds for
q_{2}\left(a,b\right)
,

\partial_{1}q_{1}\left(a,b\right),

\partial_{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)
,

\partial_{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)
,
\partial_{1}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)
,

\partial_{11}^{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)
,
\partial_{22}^{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)
.

We consider
b\partial_{1}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)
. Let
f\left(\omega\right)

be the probability density of
\omega_{21}
:

f\left(\omega\right)=\frac{1}{Z}\left(1-\omega^{2}\right)^{\left(d_{2}-3\right)/2}\mathbb{I}\left(\left|\omega\right|\leq 1\right),

where
Z
is a normalization factor. We state a few simple properties:
f

is continuous and supported on
\left[-1,1\right]
and differentiable
on
\left(-1,1\right)
,
f\left(1\right)=f\left(-1\right)=0
,
f

is an even function, and
f
is non-increasing on
\left[0,1\right]
.
Then by integration by parts,

\int_{-1}^{1}\left|\omega f^{\prime}\left(\omega\right)\right|{\rm d}\omega=-2\int_{0}^{1}\omega f^{\prime}\left(\omega\right){\rm d}\omega=2\int_{0}^{1}f\left(\omega\right){\rm d}\omega=\int_{-1}^{1}f\left(\omega\right){\rm d}\omega=1.

We also have, by integration by parts,

\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{21}}\left\{\kappa b\omega_{21}\sigma^{\prime}\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega_{21}\right)\right\}=-\int_{-1}^{1}\left(f\left(\omega\right)+\omega f^{\prime}\left(\omega\right)\right)\sigma\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega\right){\rm d}\omega.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|b\partial_{1}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\kappa^{2}b\omega_{21}\omega_{11}\sigma^{\prime}\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega_{21}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\kappa\omega_{11}\int_{-1}^{1}\left(f\left(\omega\right)+\omega f^{\prime}\left(\omega\right)\right)\sigma\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega\right){\rm d}\omega\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\left|\kappa\omega_{11}\right|\right\}\int_{-1}^{1}\left(f\left(\omega\right)+\left|\omega f^{\prime}\left(\omega\right)\right|\right){\rm d}\omega\leq C.

A similar argument applies to
a\partial_{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)
,

b\partial_{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)
,
a\partial_{1}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)
,

a\partial_{22}^{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)
,
b\partial_{11}^{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)
.

Next we consider
\left(1/a\right)\cdot q_{1}\left(a,b\right)
:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{a}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\frac{1}{a}\kappa\omega_{11}\sigma\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega_{21}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{2}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\frac{1}{a}\kappa\omega_{11}\left(\sigma\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega_{21}\right)-\sigma\left(-\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega_{21}\right)\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{=}}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\kappa^{2}\omega_{11}^{2}\sigma^{\prime}\left(\kappa a\zeta+\kappa b\omega_{21}\right)\right\}\right|\leq C,

where we have used the fact that
\omega_{11}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}-\omega_{11}

independent of
\omega_{21}
in step
\left(a\right)
and the mean
value theorem, for some
\zeta
that lies between
-\omega_{11}

and
\omega_{11}
, in step
\left(b\right)
. The same argument applies
to
\left(1/b\right)\cdot q_{2}\left(a,b\right)
.

We consider
\left(b/a\right)\cdot\partial_{1}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)
,
whose treatment is a combination of previously used arguments. In
particular,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{b}{a}\partial_{1}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\frac{b}{a}\kappa^{2}\omega_{11}\omega_{21}\sigma^{\prime}\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega_{21}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{=}}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\frac{1}{a}\kappa\omega_{11}\int_{-1}^{1}\left(f\left(\omega\right)+\omega f^{\prime}\left(\omega\right)\right)\sigma\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega\right){\rm d}\omega\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{2}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\frac{1}{a}\kappa\omega_{11}\int_{-1}^{1}\left(f\left(\omega\right)+\omega f^{\prime}\left(\omega\right)\right)\left(\sigma\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega\right)-\sigma\left(-\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega\right)\right){\rm d}\omega\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{=}}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\kappa^{2}\omega_{11}^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\left(f\left(\omega\right)+\omega f^{\prime}\left(\omega\right)\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\kappa a\zeta+\kappa b\omega\right){\rm d}\omega\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(d\right)}}{{\leq}}C,

where we use the integration-by-parts formula in step
\left(a\right)
,
the fact that
\omega_{11}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}-\omega_{11}
independent
of
\omega_{21}
in step
\left(b\right)
, the mean value theorem
in step
\left(c\right)
, and the same argument as in the bounding
of
\left|b\partial_{1}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|
in step
\left(d\right)
.
The same argument applies to
\left(a/b\right)\cdot\partial_{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)
.

Finally we consider
b\partial_{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)
. We have:

\displaystyle\left|b\partial_{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\kappa^{2}b\omega_{21}^{2}\sigma^{\prime}\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega_{21}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{=}}\left|-2q_{2}\left(a,b\right)+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\int_{-1}^{1}\kappa\omega^{2}f^{\prime}\left(\omega\right)\sigma\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega\right)\right\}{\rm d}\omega\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{\leq}}C+\left|\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\kappa^{3}\frac{\omega_{21}^{3}}{1-\omega_{21}^{2}}\sigma\left(\kappa a\omega_{11}+\kappa b\omega_{21}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C+C\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\kappa^{6}\omega_{21}^{6}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\left(1-\omega_{21}^{2}\right)^{-2}\right\}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{\leq}}C,

where in step
\left(a\right)
, we apply integration by parts; in
step
\left(b\right)
, we use
f^{\prime}\left(\omega\right)/f\left(\omega\right)=\left(d_{2}-3\right)\omega/\left[2\left(1-\omega^{2}\right)\right]

for
\left|\omega\right|<1
and that
\kappa=\sqrt{d}
; in step

\left(c\right)
, we use the bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\left(1-\omega_{21}^{2}\right)^{-2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d_{2}}g_{i}^{2}\right)^{2}\left(\sum_{i=2}^{d_{2}}g_{i}^{2}\right)^{-2}\right\}\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d_{2}}g_{i}^{2}\right)^{4}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\left\{\left(\sum_{i=2}^{d_{2}}g_{i}^{2}\right)^{-4}\right\}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\Gamma\left(d_{2}/2+4\right)}{\Gamma\left(d_{2}/2\right)}\times\frac{\Gamma\left(\left(d_{2}-1\right)/2-4\right)}{\Gamma\left(\left(d_{2}-1\right)/2\right)}}\leq C,

for
\left(g_{i}\right)_{i\leq d_{2}}\sim_{{\rm i.i.d.}}\mathsf{N}\left(0,1\right)

and
d_{2}>9
. Similar arguments apply to
a\partial_{1}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)
,

a\partial_{11}^{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)
,
b\partial_{22}^{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)
,

a\partial_{12}^{2}q_{1}\left(a,b\right)
and
b\partial_{12}^{2}q_{2}\left(a,b\right)

∎

Proposition 25
.

Consider setting
[S.2
,
and
\rho={\rm Law}\left(r_{1}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1},r_{2}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\right)

in which
\left(r_{1},r_{2}\right)
,
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}
and

\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}
are mutually independent,
\left(r_{1},r_{2}\right)\sim\rho_{r}
,

r_{1},r_{2}\geq 0
and
\int\left(r_{1}+r_{2}\right){\rm d}\rho_{r}\leq C
.
Then:

•

The following growth bounds hold:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}\right)-\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1};\rho\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2};\rho\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}.

Furthermore,
\left|V\left(\boldsymbol{0}\right)\right|=\left|U\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{0}\right)\right|=\left|W\left(\boldsymbol{0};\rho^{\prime}\right)\right|=0

for any
\rho^{\prime}
.

•

We also have:

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\left(\boldsymbol{x}\right)\equiv\int\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\rho\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\int\left(r_{1}q_{1}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{2}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}},\quad r_{2}q_{2}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{2}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}}\right)\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right),

for any
\boldsymbol{x}=\left(\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]},\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right)
,
and
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
are as defined in (
21
)
and (
22
). Furthermore, for any
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\kappa\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\left(\boldsymbol{x}\right),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|^{8}\right\}\leq C
.

Proof.

The proof comprises of several parts.

Bounds for
V
.

We have:

V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{-\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}+\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}=-\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\sigma\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)\right\}+\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

We calculate
\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
and
\nabla^{2}V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
:

\displaystyle\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)+g^{2}\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)\right\}+2\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta},

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{\left(1+g^{2}\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)\right\}+2\lambda\boldsymbol{I}_{d}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{\left(1+g^{2}\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)\right\}.

Since
\left\|\sigma^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C
and
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C
,
it is easy to see that
\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}
.
We also have from Stein’s lemma:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{g\left(2g-g^{3}\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{\left(2-3g^{2}\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left(2g-g^{3}\right)\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{-\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)-3\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left(2g-g^{3}\right)\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{-\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)-\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\left(1+g^{2}\right)\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)\right\},

and thus, using the fact
\left\|\sigma^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C
:

\left|\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\left(1+g^{2}\right)\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)\right\}\right|=\left|\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{\left(g\left(2-g^{3}\right)+1\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}g\right)\right\}\right|\leq C.

It is then easy to see that
\left\|\nabla^{2}V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C
,
since
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\geq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}
.
This in particular implies

\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}\right)-\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\right\|_{2},

as desired.

Bounds for
W
.

Let us define
\chi_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\Sigma_{1}\sqrt{\alpha/d_{1}}Z_{1}

and
\chi_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\Sigma_{2}\sqrt{\left(1-\alpha\right)/d_{2}}Z_{2}

two independent random variables, which are independent of
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}

and
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}
, where
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
are respectively

\chi
-random variables of degrees of freedom
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
.
For ease of presentation, let us introduce several notations, for

j,i,k\in\left\{1,2\right\}
:

\displaystyle q_{j}^{r}

\displaystyle=q_{j}\left(r_{1}\chi_{1},r_{2}\chi_{2}\right),

\displaystyle q_{j}^{\theta}

\displaystyle=q_{j}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}\chi_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}\chi_{2}\right),

\displaystyle\partial_{i}q_{j}^{\theta}

\displaystyle=\partial_{i}q_{j}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}\chi_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}\chi_{2}\right),

\displaystyle\partial_{ik}^{2}q_{j}^{\theta}

\displaystyle=\partial_{ik}^{2}q_{j}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}\chi_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}\chi_{2}\right).

The meaning of each particular quantity shall be clear in the context
it is used.

We first do a useful calculation. For a fixed vector
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}

and any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
,
a\geq 0
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\sigma\left(a\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\right\rangle+b\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\left(\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{v}+{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\right)\sigma\left(a\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\right\rangle+b\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{=}}\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\right\rangle\sigma\left(a\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\right\rangle+b\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{=}}\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\omega_{11}\sigma\left(a\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}\omega_{11}+b\right)\right\},

(28)

where step
\left(a\right)
is because conditioning on
\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\right\rangle
,
we have
{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}-{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}
;
step
\left(b\right)
follows from that
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}

for any orthogonal matrix
\boldsymbol{Q}
, and we choose
\boldsymbol{Q}

such that
\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}=\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2},0,...,0\right)^{\top}
.
Using this calculation, we have for any
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

\displaystyle\int\kappa\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\rho\left({\rm d}\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int\left(r_{1}q_{1}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{2}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}},\quad r_{2}q_{2}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{2}\right)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}}\right)\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right).

We then obtain, again by Eq. (
28
),
for
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right),\int\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\rho\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\int\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{r_{j}q_{j}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{2}\right)\frac{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]},\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[j\right]}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\int\mathbb{E}_{\chi,\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{r_{j}q_{j}^{r}\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{j}\right\rangle\sigma\left(\chi_{j}\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{j}\right\rangle+\chi_{\neg j}\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[\neg j\right]},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\neg j}\right\rangle\right)\right\}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\int r_{j}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{q_{j}^{r}q_{j}^{\theta}\right\}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right),

where we assume the convention
\neg j=2
if
j=1
and
\neg j=1

if
j=2
. We calculate
\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)
:

\displaystyle\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)_{\left[1\right]},\quad\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)_{\left[2\right]}\right),

\displaystyle\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)_{\left[j\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}}\int r_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{q_{j}^{r}q_{j}^{\theta}\right\}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)+\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\int r_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}q_{j}^{r}\partial_{j}q_{j}^{\theta}\right\}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}}\int r_{\neg j}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[\neg j\right]}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}q_{\neg j}^{r}\partial_{j}q_{\neg j}^{\theta}\right\}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right),\qquad j=1,2.

Note that
\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\chi_{j}\right|\right\}\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}^{2}\right\}}=\sqrt{\Sigma_{j}^{2}d_{j}/d}\leq C

and

\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\frac{\chi_{j}}{\chi_{\neg j}}\right|\right\}\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{\neg j}^{-2}\right\}}\leq\sqrt{\Sigma_{j}^{2}d_{j}/d}\sqrt{\Sigma_{\neg j}^{-2}d/\left(d_{\neg j}-2\right)}\leq C.

Then by Lemma
24
, along with the fact

\int\left(r_{1}+r_{2}\right){\rm d}\rho_{r}\leq C
, we have:

\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}\leq C\int r_{j}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)+C\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\frac{\chi_{j}}{\chi_{\neg j}}\right|\right\}\int r_{\neg j}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\leq C,

which implies
\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C

as desired. Next we calculate
\nabla_{11}^{2}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)
:

\displaystyle\nabla_{11}^{2}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\left[\nabla_{11}^{2}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right]_{11}&\left[\nabla_{11}^{2}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right]_{12}\\
\left[\nabla_{11}^{2}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right]_{12}^{\top}&\left[\nabla_{11}^{2}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right]_{22}\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\left[\nabla_{11}^{2}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right]_{jj}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{I}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}}-\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}^{\top}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}^{3}}\right)\int\left(r_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{q_{j}^{r}q_{j}^{\theta}\right\}+r_{\neg j}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[\neg j\right]}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}q_{\neg j}^{r}\partial_{j}q_{\neg j}^{\theta}\right\}\right)\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\boldsymbol{I}+\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}^{\top}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\int r_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}q_{j}^{r}\partial_{j}q_{j}^{\theta}\right\}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}^{\top}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}}\int r_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}^{2}q_{j}^{r}\partial_{jj}^{2}q_{j}^{\theta}\right\}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}^{\top}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\int r_{\neg j}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[\neg j\right]}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}^{2}q_{\neg j}^{r}\partial_{jj}^{2}q_{\neg j}^{\theta}\right\}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right),

\displaystyle\left[\nabla_{11}^{2}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right]_{12}

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[1\right]}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[2\right]}^{\top}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}}\Bigg(\int\left(r_{1}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{2}q_{1}^{r}\partial_{2}q_{1}^{\theta}\right\}+r_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{1}q_{2}^{r}\partial_{1}q_{2}^{\theta}\right\}\right)\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{1}\chi_{2}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{1}q_{1}^{r}\partial_{12}^{2}q_{1}^{\theta}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{2}q_{2}^{r}\partial_{12}^{2}q_{2}^{\theta}\right)\right\}\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\Bigg).

Then again by Lemma
24
, along with the
fact
\int\left(r_{1}+r_{2}\right){\rm d}\rho_{r}\leq C
, we have:

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\left[\nabla_{11}^{2}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right]_{jj}\boldsymbol{b}\right\rangle\right|\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2},\qquad\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{a}_{1},\left[\nabla_{11}^{2}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right]_{12}\boldsymbol{a}_{2}\right\rangle\right|\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{a}_{1}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}_{2}\right\|_{2},

for any
\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{j}}
and

\boldsymbol{a}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
,
\boldsymbol{a}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
.
This implies
\left\|\nabla_{11}^{2}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C
,
which shows that

\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1};\rho\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2};\rho\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\right\|_{2}.

Bounds for
U
.

Now we consider
U
:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)+\kappa^{3}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}+\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\kappa^{3}\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|\right\}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}+\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{\left|g\right|\right\}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}.

This shows that
\left\|\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\kappa^{2}\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}
.

Statement at
\boldsymbol{0}
.

It is easy to see that
V\left(\boldsymbol{0}\right)=U\left(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{0}\right)=W\left(\boldsymbol{0};\rho^{\prime}\right)=0

for any
\rho^{\prime}
.

Statement on
\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\left(\boldsymbol{x}\right)
.

The formula for
\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\left(\boldsymbol{x}\right)

is shown in the bounding for
W
. Defining

s_{j}=\int r_{j}q_{j}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{2}\right)\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right),\qquad j=1,2,

we have
\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\left(\boldsymbol{x}\right)=\left(s_{1}\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}/\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2},\;s_{2}\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}/\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}\right)
.
By Lemma
24
, along with the fact
\int\left(r_{1}+r_{2}\right){\rm d}\rho_{r}\leq C
,
it is easy to see that
\left|s_{1}\right|,\left|s_{2}\right|\leq C
.
Hence for any
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\kappa\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\left(\boldsymbol{x}\right),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|^{8}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\kappa\left\langle\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\right\rangle\right|^{8}+\left|\kappa\left\langle\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\right\rangle\right|^{8}\right\}

\displaystyle=C\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\left|\kappa\left\langle\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\right\rangle\right|^{8}+\left|\kappa\left\langle\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\right\rangle\right|^{8}\right\}\leq C,

by Lemma
23
.

∎

Proposition 26
.

Consider setting
[S.2

and, for each
k=1,2
, consider
\rho_{k}={\rm Law}\left(r_{1,k}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1},r_{2,k}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\right)

in which
\left(r_{1,k},r_{2,k}\right)
,
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}

and
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}
are mutually independent,
\left(r_{1,k},r_{2,k}\right)\sim\rho_{r,k}
,

r_{1,k},r_{2,k}\geq 0
and
\int\left(r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}\right)\rho_{r,k}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\leq C
.
Then:

\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{1}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{2}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\rho_{r,1},\rho_{r,2}\right),

where
\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\rho_{r,1},\rho_{r,2}\right)
is the
Wasserstein distance given as:

\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\rho_{r,1},\rho_{r,2}\right)=\inf\left\{\int\left\|\boldsymbol{r}_{1}-\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\right\|_{2}^{2}\nu\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{r}_{1},{\rm d}\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\right):\;\boldsymbol{r}_{k}\sim\rho_{r,k},\;k=1,2,\;\nu\text{ a coupling of }\rho_{r,1}\text{ and }\rho_{r,2}\right\}^{1/2}.

Proof.

We have the following formula given in the proof of Proposition
25
,
for
k=1,2
:

\displaystyle\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{k}\right)_{\left[1\right]},\quad\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{k}\right)_{\left[2\right]}\right),

\displaystyle\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{k}\right)_{\left[j\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}}\int r_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{q_{j}^{r}q_{j}^{\theta}\right\}\rho_{r,k}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)+\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\int r_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}q_{j}^{r}\partial_{j}q_{j}^{\theta}\right\}\rho_{r,k}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}}\int r_{\neg j}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[\neg j\right]}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}q_{\neg j}^{r}\partial_{j}q_{\neg j}^{\theta}\right\}\rho_{r,k}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right),\qquad j=1,2,

where we recall the short-hand notations, for
j,i\in\left\{1,2\right\}
:

q_{j}^{r}=q_{j}\left(r_{1}\chi_{1},r_{2}\chi_{2}\right),\quad q_{j}^{\theta}=q_{j}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}\chi_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}\chi_{2}\right),\quad\partial_{i}q_{j}^{\theta}=\partial_{i}q_{j}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}\chi_{1},\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}\chi_{2}\right).

Here
\chi_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\Sigma_{1}\sqrt{\alpha/d_{1}}Z_{1}

and
\chi_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\Sigma_{2}\sqrt{\left(1-\alpha\right)/d_{2}}Z_{2}

are two independent random variables, which are independent of
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}

and
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}
, where
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
are respectively

\chi
-random variables of degrees of freedom
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
.
By Lemma
24
,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{1}\right)_{\left[1\right]}-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{2}\right)_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left|\int r_{1}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{q_{1}^{r}q_{1}^{\theta}\right\}\left(\rho_{r,1}-\rho_{r,2}\right)\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\right|+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}\left|\int r_{1}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{1}q_{1}^{r}\partial_{1}q_{1}^{\theta}\right\}\left(\rho_{r,1}-\rho_{r,2}\right)\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}\left|\int r_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{1}q_{2}^{r}\partial_{1}q_{2}^{\theta}\right\}\left(\rho_{r,1}-\rho_{r,2}\right)\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\int r_{1}q_{1}^{r}\left(\rho_{r,1}-\rho_{r,2}\right)\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\right|+\frac{\chi_{1}}{\chi_{2}}\left|\int r_{2}q_{2}^{r}\left(\rho_{r,1}-\rho_{r,2}\right)\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\right|\right\}.

Let us consider
\left|\int r_{1}q_{1}^{r}\left(\rho_{r,1}-\rho_{r,2}\right)\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\right|
.
Consider any coupling between
\rho_{r,1}
and
\rho_{r,2}
so that
we can place
\left(r_{1,1},r_{2,1}\right)\sim\rho_{r,1}
and
\left(r_{1,2},r_{2,2}\right)\sim\rho_{r,2}

on the same joint probability space. Let
\mathbb{E}_{\backslash\chi}

denote the expectation w.r.t. these random variables, excluding
\chi_{1}

and
\chi_{2}
. We have:

\displaystyle\left|\int r_{1}q_{1}^{r}\left(\rho_{r,1}-\rho_{r,2}\right)\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{\backslash\chi}\left\{\left|r_{1,1}q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,1},\chi_{2}r_{2,1}\right)-r_{1,2}q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,2},\chi_{2}r_{2,2}\right)\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}_{\backslash\chi}\left\{\left|q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,1},\chi_{2}r_{2,1}\right)\right|\left|r_{1,1}-r_{1,2}\right|\right\}+\mathbb{E}_{\backslash\chi}\left\{r_{1,2}\left|q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,1},\chi_{2}r_{2,1}\right)-q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,2},\chi_{2}r_{2,1}\right)\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{\backslash\chi}\left\{r_{1,2}\left|q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,2},\chi_{2}r_{2,1}\right)-q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,2},\chi_{2}r_{2,2}\right)\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}_{\backslash\chi}\left\{\left|q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,1},\chi_{2}r_{2,1}\right)\right|\left|r_{1,1}-r_{1,2}\right|\right\}+\chi_{1}\mathbb{E}_{\backslash\chi}\left\{r_{1,2}\left|\partial_{1}q_{1}\left(\zeta_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2,1}\right)\right|\left|r_{1,1}-r_{1,2}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\chi_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\backslash\chi}\left\{r_{1,2}\left|\partial_{2}q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,2},\zeta_{2}\right)\right|\left|r_{2,1}-r_{2,2}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{\leq}}C\left(1+\left(\chi_{1}+\chi_{2}\right)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\backslash\chi}\left\{r_{1,2}^{2}\right\}}\right)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\backslash\chi}\left\{\left|r_{1,1}-r_{1,2}\right|^{2}+\left|r_{2,1}-r_{2,2}\right|^{2}\right\}}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{\leq}}C\left(1+\chi_{1}+\chi_{2}\right)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\backslash\chi}\left\{\left|r_{1,1}-r_{1,2}\right|^{2}+\left|r_{2,1}-r_{2,2}\right|^{2}\right\}}

where in step
\left(a\right)
, we use the mean value theorem for
some
\zeta_{1}
between
\chi_{1}r_{1,1}
and
\chi_{1}r_{1,2}

and some
\zeta_{2}
between
\chi_{2}r_{2,1}
and
\chi_{2}r_{2,2}
;
in step
\left(b\right)
, we apply Lemma
24
;
in step
\left(c\right)
, we recall the assumption
\int\left(r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}\right)\rho_{r,k}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\leq C

for
k=1,2
. Since the coupling is arbitrary, we have:

\left|\int r_{1}q_{1}^{r}\left(\rho_{r,1}-\rho_{r,2}\right)\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\right|\leq C\left(1+\chi_{1}+\chi_{2}\right)\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\rho_{r,1},\rho_{r,2}\right).

We treat
\left|\int r_{2}q_{2}^{r}\left(\rho_{r,1}-\rho_{r,2}\right)\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right)\right|

similarly and then obtain:

\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{1}\right)_{\left[1\right]}-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{2}\right)_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}\leq C\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\rho_{r,1},\rho_{r,2}\right).

A similar bound holds for
\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{1}\right)_{\left[2\right]}-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta};\rho_{2}\right)_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}
.
The thesis then follows.
∎

Lemma 27
.

Assume an activation
\sigma

as described in setting
[S.2
, and a bounded
function
\phi:\;\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
,
\left\|\phi\right\|_{\infty}\leq K
.
Let
w\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,s^{2}\right)
. Then for any integer
m\geq 0

and any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{w}\left\{w^{m}\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\phi\left(w\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq KC\left(m+1\right)^{\left(m+1\right)/2}s^{m-1},

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{w}\left\{w^{m}\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\phi\left(w\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq KC\left(m+1\right)^{\left(m+1\right)/2}s^{m-1},

where
C
is a constant that is independent of
K
,
s
and
m
.

Proof.

By assumption, there exists an anti-derivative
\hat{\sigma}_{2}

of
\left|\sigma^{\prime\prime}\right|
such that
\left\|\hat{\sigma}_{2}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C
.
Let
f
be the standard Gaussian probability density function. For
any integer
m\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{w}\left\{w^{m}\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\phi\left(w\right)\right\}\right|\leq KC\mathbb{E}_{w}\left\{\left|w\right|^{m}\left|\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\right|\right\}=KCs^{m}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\left|u\right|^{m}\left|\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(su\right)\right|f\left(u\right){\rm d}u

\displaystyle\qquad=KCs^{m-1}\left(\left[\left|u\right|^{m}\hat{\sigma}_{2}\left(su\right)f\left(u\right)\right]_{u=-\infty}^{+\infty}-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\hat{\sigma}_{2}\left(su\right)\left(m\left|u\right|^{m-1}{\rm sign}\left(u\right)-u\left|u\right|^{m}\right)f\left(u\right){\rm d}u\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq KCs^{m-1}\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\{m\left|g\right|^{m-1}+\left|g\right|^{m+1}\right\}\leq KC\left(m+1\right)^{\left(m+1\right)/2}s^{m-1}.

The proof for the second statement is similar.
∎

Proposition 28
.

Consider setting
[S.2
.
We have:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{121}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}},\left\|\nabla_{122}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{12}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\right)\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2},

for any
\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Proof.

The proof is almost the same as that of Proposition
18
,
so we omit several similar calculations and refer to the proof of
Proposition
18
for the definitions of
the quantities. In particular, we obtain:

\displaystyle\left|A_{1}\right|,\left|A_{2}\right|,\left|A_{4}\right|,\left|A_{5}\right|,\left|B_{1}\right|,\left|B_{3}\right|,\left|B_{4}\right|,\left|B_{5}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left|F_{1}\right|,\left|F_{2}\right|,\left|F_{3}\right|,\left|F_{4}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\right)\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left|H_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2},

for a suitable constant
C
. We are left with
A_{3}
,
A_{6}
,

B_{2}
,
B_{6}
and
H_{2}
. We consider
A_{3}
. Proceeding
as in the proof of Proposition
18
, we
have:

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle+\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}w\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{c},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle+\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}w\right)\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{c},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle+\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}w\right)\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle+\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}w\right)\frac{w^{2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{4}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right\},

for
\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)

and
w\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)

independently, where
\boldsymbol{S}={\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
.
Applying Lemma
27
, recalling that

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C
,
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}\geq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{S}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C
,
we obtain:

\displaystyle\left|A_{3}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{c},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\kappa^{2}\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left(\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{c},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right|\right\}+\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{a},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle\right|\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\kappa^{2}\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{3}}\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2}.

Similar calculations yield:

\displaystyle\left|A_{3}\right|,\left|A_{6}\right|,\left|B_{2}\right|,\left|B_{6}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}+1\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left|H_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}.

The thesis then follows.
∎

Proposition 29
.

Consider setting
[S.2
.
Recall the process
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t},\rho_{r}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

that is described as in the statement of Theorem
15

via the ODE (
23
). This ODE has a (weakly)
unique solution on
t\in[0,\infty)
. Furthermore, this solution satisfies
a sub-Gaussian moment bound:

\int\left(\bar{r}_{1}^{p}+\bar{r}_{2}^{p}\right)\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\leq C^{p}\left(1+t^{p}\right)p^{p/2},

for any integer
p\geq 1
, where the immaterial constant
C
is independent
of
t
. We also have,
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t}\right)
is a deterministic
function of
\left(r_{1,0},r_{2,0}\right)
, i.e.
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t}\right)=\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,0},r_{2,0}\right)
,
such that
\left\|\partial_{t}\psi_{t}\left(r_{1},r_{2}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\left(1+t+r_{1}+r_{2}\right)
.

Proof.

We decompose the proof into several steps. We first show existence
and uniqueness of the solution, via a Picard-type iteration argument,
by adapting the strategy of
[
Szn91
]
. This is done
from Steps 1-3 below. Then we show the properties of the solution.
Before we proceed, let us define:

\displaystyle G_{j}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\rho\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\rho\right)\left[q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)+\chi_{j}r_{j}\partial_{j}q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{\neg j}\left(\chi,\rho\right)\chi_{j}r_{\neg j}\partial_{j}q_{\neg j}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)\right\}-2\lambda r_{j},\qquad j=1,2,

where we recall the convention
\neg j=2
if
j=1
and
\neg j=1

if
j=2
.

Step 1: Setup.

Fix a terminal time
T\geq 0
that is to be chosen later. Let
{\cal C}={\cal C}\left(\left[0,T\right];\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)

be the set of continuous mappings from
\left[0,T\right]
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
and
\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
the set of probability measures
on
{\cal C}
such that if
\mu\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
,

\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}\leq K

for
j=1,2
and any
t\in\left[0,T\right]
, for a constant
K\geq 0

that is to be chosen later. We equip this space with the following
Wasserstein metric:

\mathscr{W}_{T}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right)=\inf\left\{\int\sup_{t\leq T}\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left(r_{j,t}^{\left(1\right)}-r_{j,t}^{\left(2\right)}\right)^{2}\nu\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{r}^{\left(1\right)},{\rm d}\boldsymbol{r}^{\left(2\right)}\right):\;\text{$\nu$ is a coupling of $\mu_{1}$ and $\mu_{2}$}\right\}^{1/2}.

Note that this defines a complete metric on
\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};\infty\right)
.
We also note
\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)\subseteq\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};\infty\right)

for all
K\geq 0
. We prove that
\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)

is still a complete metric space under
\mathscr{W}_{T}
. Observe
that, for any
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};\infty\right)

and
t\in\left[0,T\right]
,

\displaystyle\left|\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{1}^{t}\right)-\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{2}^{t}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{r_{1,t}^{\left(1\right)}q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,t}^{\left(1\right)},\chi_{2}r_{2,t}^{\left(1\right)}\right)-r_{1,t}^{\left(2\right)}q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,t}^{\left(2\right)},\chi_{2}r_{2,t}^{\left(2\right)}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{u_{1},u_{2}\geq 0}\left|q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}u_{1},\chi_{2}u_{2}\right)+\chi_{1}u_{1}\partial_{1}q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}u_{1},\chi_{2}u_{2}\right)\right|\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|r_{1,t}^{\left(1\right)}-r_{1,t}^{\left(2\right)}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\chi_{2}/\chi_{1}\right)\sup_{u_{1},u_{2}\geq 0}\left|\chi_{1}u_{1}\partial_{2}q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}u_{1},\chi_{2}u_{2}\right)\right|\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|r_{2,t}^{\left(1\right)}-r_{2,t}^{\left(2\right)}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{\leq}}C\left(1+\chi_{2}/\chi_{1}\right)\left(\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|r_{1,t}^{\left(1\right)}-r_{1,t}^{\left(2\right)}\right|\right\}+\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|r_{2,t}^{\left(1\right)}-r_{2,t}^{\left(2\right)}\right|\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\chi_{2}/\chi_{1}\right)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(r_{1,t}^{\left(1\right)}-r_{1,t}^{\left(2\right)}\right)^{2}+\left(r_{2,t}^{\left(1\right)}-r_{2,t}^{\left(2\right)}\right)^{2}\right\}},

(29)

where the expectation is taken over an arbitrary coupling between

\left(r_{1,t}^{\left(1\right)},r_{2,t}^{\left(1\right)}\right)\sim\mu_{1}^{t}

and
\left(r_{1,t}^{\left(2\right)},r_{2,t}^{\left(2\right)}\right)\sim\mu_{2}^{t}
,
and step
\left(a\right)
is due to Lemma
24
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{1}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{2}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{1}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{2}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}\right)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{1}^{t}\right)-\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{2}^{t}\right)\right|^{2}\right\}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{1}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{2}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}\right)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{1+\chi_{2}^{2}/\chi_{1}^{2}\right\}}\mathscr{W}_{T}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{1}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{2}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}\right)\mathscr{W}_{T}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right).

Now we take a sequence
\left(\mu_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such
that
\mu_{n}\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
and
\mu_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathscr{W}_{T}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mu
,
and apply this result to
\mu_{n}
and
\mu
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{n}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}+C\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{n}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}\right)\mathscr{W}_{T}\left(\mu_{n},\mu\right)

\displaystyle\leq K+C\left(\sqrt{K}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}\right)\mathscr{W}_{T}\left(\mu_{n},\mu\right),

since
\mu_{n}\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
. Suppose that

\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}\geq K+\epsilon

for an arbitrary
\epsilon>0
and some
t\in\left[0,T\right]
. Then
the above implies,

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{K+C\sqrt{K}\mathscr{W}_{T}\left(\mu_{n},\mu\right)}{\sqrt{K+\epsilon}-C\mathscr{W}_{T}\left(\mu_{n},\mu\right)}=\frac{K}{\sqrt{K+\epsilon}},

which contradicts
\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}\geq K+\epsilon
.
Hence
\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}\leq K
.
We also have similarly
\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{2}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}\leq K
.
That is,
\mu\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
, and hence
\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)

is closed. Since
\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)\subseteq\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};\infty\right)

and
\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};\infty\right)
is complete, we have
that
\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
is complete, as desired.

Step 2: The iterating map
\Phi
.

We shall depart from the initial law
\rho_{r}^{0}
as given in the
ODE (
23
), and consider a generic initial
law
\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\in\mathscr{P}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)

such that
M\left(\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right)<\infty
, where we define

M\left(\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right)=\max\left(1,\;\int\left(\bar{r}_{1}^{2}+\bar{r}_{2}^{2}\right)\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\right).

Define
\Phi:\;\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)\to\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)

which associates
\mu\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
to the
law of
\left(\tilde{r}_{1,t},\tilde{r}_{2,t}\right)_{t\in\left[0,T\right]}
,
which is the solution to

\tilde{r}_{j,t}=\tilde{r}_{j,0}+\int_{s=0}^{t}G_{j}\left(\tilde{r}_{1,s},\tilde{r}_{2,s},\mu^{s}\right){\rm d}s,\qquad t\leq T,\quad j=1,2,\quad\left(\tilde{r}_{1,0},\tilde{r}_{2,0}\right)\sim\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}.

If
\mu
is a weak solution of the ODE (
23
)
with initialization
\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}
, then it is a fixed point
of
\Phi
, and vice versa – assuming that this is well-defined.
That is, we need to check that firstly, the process
\left(\tilde{r}_{1,t},\tilde{r}_{2,t}\right)_{t\in\left[0,T\right]}

under
\mu\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
exists and is unique
under any initialization
\left(\tilde{r}_{1,0},\tilde{r}_{2,0}\right)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)
,
and secondly,
\Phi\left(\mu\right)\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)

for any
\mu\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
, for suitably
chosen
K
and
T
. We remark that
\Phi\left(\mu\right)\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)

already implies
\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right)^{2}\right\}\leq K

for
j\in\left\{1,2\right\}
.

We check the first condition. By Lemma
24
,
for
\mu\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
and any
t\leq T
,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{1}G_{1}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu^{t}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\Big|-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\left[\chi_{1}\partial_{1}q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)+\chi_{1}^{2}r_{1}\partial_{11}^{2}q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{2}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\chi_{1}^{2}r_{2}\partial_{11}^{2}q_{2}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)\right\}-2\lambda\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{1}\left|\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\right|+\frac{\chi_{1}^{2}}{\chi_{2}}\left|\Delta_{2}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\right|+1\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{1}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\right|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{1}^{4}/\chi_{2}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\Delta_{2}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\right|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sqrt{K}+1\right),

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{2}G_{1}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu^{t}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\Big|-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\left[\chi_{2}\partial_{2}q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)+\chi_{1}\chi_{2}r_{1}\partial_{12}^{2}q_{1}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{2}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\chi_{1}\chi_{2}r_{2}\partial_{12}^{2}q_{2}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)\right\}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{2}\left|\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\right|+\chi_{1}\left|\Delta_{2}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{2}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\right|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{1}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\Delta_{2}\left(\chi,\mu^{t}\right)\right|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{K}.

Similarly
\left|\partial_{2}G_{2}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu^{t}\right)\right|,\;\left|\partial_{1}G_{2}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu^{t}\right)\right|\leq C\left(\sqrt{K}+1\right)
,
uniformly in
t\in\left[0,T\right]
. It is easy to see that
t\mapsto G_{j}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu^{t}\right)

is continuous, for
j\in\left\{1,2\right\}
and any
r_{1}
,
r_{2}
,
since
\mu\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
. Existence and uniqueness
of
\left(\tilde{r}_{1,t},\tilde{r}_{2,t}\right)_{t\in\left[0,T\right]}

then follow upon choosing
K<\infty
.

We check the second condition. We have for
\mu\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
:

\displaystyle\tilde{r}_{1,t}

\displaystyle=\tilde{r}_{1,0}+\int_{s=0}^{t}\left(G_{1}\left(\tilde{r}_{1,s},\tilde{r}_{2,s},\mu^{s}\right)+2\lambda\tilde{r}_{1,s}\right){\rm d}s-\int_{s=0}^{t}2\lambda\tilde{r}_{1,s}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{\leq}}\tilde{r}_{1,0}+C\int_{s=0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{s}\right)\right|+\left(\chi_{1}/\chi_{2}\right)\left|\Delta_{2}\left(\chi,\mu^{s}\right)\right|\right\}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{r}_{1,0}+C\int_{s=0}^{t}\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu^{s}\right)\right|^{2}\right\}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{1}^{2}/\chi_{2}^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\Delta_{2}\left(\chi,\mu^{s}\right)\right|^{2}\right\}}\right){\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{r}_{1,0}+C\sqrt{K}t

(30)

where step
\left(a\right)
is due to Lemma
24

and the fact
\lambda\tilde{r}_{1,s}\geq 0
. Using this and recalling
that
\Phi\left(\mu\right)^{t}={\rm Law}\left(\tilde{r}_{1,t},\tilde{r}_{2,t}\right)
,
we get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\Phi\left(\mu\right)^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\int\bar{r}_{1}^{2}\Phi\left(\mu\right)^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)+2\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{1}^{2}\right\}=C\mathbb{E}\left\{\tilde{r}_{j,t}^{2}\right\}+C

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left\{\tilde{r}_{1,0}^{2}\right\}+CKt^{2}+C\leq C\left(M\left(\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right)+KT^{2}\right),

where we have used Lemma
24
in the first
inequality and the fact
M\left(\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right)\geq 1

by definition. One can obtain similarly:

\max_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\Phi\left(\mu\right)^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}\leq C_{*}\left(M\left(\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right)+KT^{2}\right),

for some constant
C_{*}>0
independent of
M\left(\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right)
,

K
and
T
. By choosing
K=2C_{*}M\left(\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right)<\infty

and
T=1/\sqrt{2C_{*}}
, we get
\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\Phi\left(\mu\right)^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}\leq K

for
j=1,2
. That is,
\Phi\left(\mu\right)\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
.

Step 3: Contraction of
\Phi
.

Now we show a contraction property of
\Phi
. Let us consider
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
,
and a coupling:

\tilde{r}_{j,t}^{\left(1\right)}=\tilde{r}_{j,0}+\int_{s=0}^{t}G_{j}\left(\tilde{r}_{1,s}^{\left(1\right)},\tilde{r}_{2,s}^{\left(1\right)},\mu_{1}^{s}\right){\rm d}s,\qquad\tilde{r}_{j,t}^{\left(2\right)}=\tilde{r}_{j,0}+\int_{s=0}^{t}G_{j}\left(\tilde{r}_{1,s}^{\left(2\right)},\tilde{r}_{2,s}^{\left(2\right)},\mu_{2}^{s}\right){\rm d}s,\qquad t\leq T,\quad j=1,2.

We have for
t\leq T
:

\displaystyle\sup_{s\leq t}\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left|\tilde{r}_{j,s}^{\left(1\right)}-\tilde{r}_{j,s}^{\left(2\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\int_{s=0}^{t}\left|G_{j}\left(\tilde{r}_{1,s}^{\left(1\right)},\tilde{r}_{2,s}^{\left(1\right)},\mu_{1}^{s}\right)-G_{j}\left(\tilde{r}_{1,s}^{\left(2\right)},\tilde{r}_{2,s}^{\left(2\right)},\mu_{2}^{s}\right)\right|{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\sum_{i\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\sup_{r_{1},r_{2}\geq 0,\;\mu\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right),\;t\leq T}\left|\partial_{i}G_{j}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu^{t}\right)\right|\int_{s=0}^{t}\left|\tilde{r}_{i,s}^{\left(1\right)}-\tilde{r}_{i,s}^{\left(2\right)}\right|{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\int_{s=0}^{t}\sup_{r_{1},r_{2}\geq 0}\left|G_{j}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu_{1}^{s}\right)-G_{j}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu_{2}^{s}\right)\right|{\rm d}s.

We recall
\left|\partial_{i}G_{j}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu^{t}\right)\right|\leq C\left(\sqrt{K}+1\right)

for
i,j\in\left\{1,2\right\}
,
t\in\left[0,T\right]
and
\mu\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)

as shown in the previous step. We also have from Eq. (
29
)
and Lemma
24
that

\displaystyle\left|G_{1}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu_{1}^{s}\right)-G_{1}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu_{2}^{s}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left|\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{1}^{s}\right)-\Delta_{1}\left(\chi,\mu_{2}^{s}\right)\right|\right\}+\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left(\chi_{2}/\chi_{1}\right)\left|\Delta_{2}\left(\chi,\mu_{1}^{s}\right)-\Delta_{2}\left(\chi,\mu_{2}^{s}\right)\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{1+\chi_{2}/\chi_{1}\right\}\mathscr{W}_{s}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right)\leq C{\cal\mathscr{W}}_{s}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right).

Similarly
\left|G_{2}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu_{1}^{s}\right)-G_{2}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\mu_{2}^{s}\right)\right|\leq C\mathscr{W}_{s}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right)
.
Combining these bounds, we then obtain:

\sup_{s\leq t}\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left|\tilde{r}_{j,s}^{\left(1\right)}-\tilde{r}_{j,s}^{\left(2\right)}\right|\leq C\left(\sqrt{K}+1\right)\int_{s=0}^{t}\sum_{i\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left|\tilde{r}_{i,s}^{\left(1\right)}-\tilde{r}_{i,s}^{\left(2\right)}\right|{\rm d}s+C\int_{s=0}^{t}{\cal\mathscr{W}}_{s}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right){\rm d}s.

Using Gronwall’s lemma:

\sup_{s\leq t}\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left|\tilde{r}_{j,s}^{\left(1\right)}-\tilde{r}_{j,s}^{\left(2\right)}\right|\leq Ce^{C\left(\sqrt{K}+1\right)T}\int_{s=0}^{t}\mathscr{W}_{s}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right){\rm d}s,

(31)

which implies

\mathscr{W}_{t}\left(\Phi\left(\mu_{1}\right),\Phi\left(\mu_{2}\right)\right)\leq Ce^{C\left(\sqrt{K}+1\right)T}\int_{s=0}^{t}\mathscr{W}_{s}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right){\rm d}s.

Iterating this result, we have for
\mu\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
:

\mathscr{W}_{T}\left(\Phi^{k}\left(\mu_{1}\right),\Phi^{k}\left(\mu_{2}\right)\right)\leq C_{T,K}^{k}\frac{T^{k}}{k!}{\cal\mathscr{W}}_{T}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right),

for any integer
k\geq 1
. Since
\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)

is complete, by substituting
\mu_{2}=\Phi\left(\mu_{1}\right)
,
this shows that
\Phi^{k}\left(\mu_{1}\right)
converges to a limit
point
\mu_{*}\in\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
as
k\to\infty
.
This limit point
\mu_{*}
is a fixed point of
\Phi
and hence
is a solution up to time
T
. The weak uniqueness of this fixed point
also follows easily. In particular, if
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
are
fixed points, then
\Phi^{k}\left(\mu_{1}\right)=\mu_{1}
and
\Phi^{k}\left(\mu_{2}\right)=\mu_{2}
.
Hence

\mathscr{W}_{T}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right)\leq C_{T,K}^{k}\frac{T^{k}}{k!}\mathscr{W}_{T}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right),

for arbitrary
k\geq 1
. This implies
{\cal\mathscr{W}}_{T}\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right)=0
.
Since
\mathscr{W}_{T}
induces the weak topology on
\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C};K\right)
,
weak uniqueness follows. Uniqueness of the solution
\left(\tilde{r}_{1,t},\tilde{r}_{2,t}\right)_{t\in\left[0,T\right]}

under
\mu_{*}
is immediate from Eq. (
31
).

We have shown the solution exists (weakly) uniquely for
t\leq T=1/\sqrt{2C_{*}}

for
C_{*}>0
independent of the initial law
\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}
.
By Eq. (
30
) and the fact
M\left(\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right)\geq 1
,
substituting the choice of
K
and
T
, we have:

M\left({\rm Law}\left(\tilde{r}_{1,T},\tilde{r}_{2,T}\right)\right)=\max\left(1,\;\mathbb{E}\left\{\tilde{r}_{1,T}^{2}+\tilde{r}_{2,T}^{2}\right\}\right)\leq CM\left(\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right)+CKT^{2}=CM\left(\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right),

which is finite if
M\left(\tilde{\rho}_{r}^{0}\right)
is finite.
Hence the existence and (weak) uniqueness of the solution can be extended
to
t\in[0,\infty)
. We now return to the original ODE (
23
).
Recall that its initial law
\rho_{r}^{0}
satisfies
M\left(\rho_{r}^{0}\right)\leq C
.
This proves the existence and (weak) uniqueness of the solution of
the ODE (
23
) on
t\in[0,\infty)
.

Step 4: Properties of
\rho_{r}^{t}
.

The above existence and uniqueness proof only shows that the law solution
lies in
\mathscr{P}\left({\cal C}\left([0,\infty),\mathbb{R}^{2}\right);\infty\right)
.
To derive its properties, we shall appeal to another approach. Consider
the following energy functional:

E\left(\rho\right)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\rho\right)^{2}\right\}+\lambda\int\left(\bar{r}_{1}^{2}+\bar{r}_{2}^{2}\right)\rho\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right).

Recall that
\left({\rm d}/{\rm d}t\right)r_{j,t}=G_{j}\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t},\rho_{r}^{t}\right)
.
We have:

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}E\left(\rho_{r}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\rho_{r}^{t}\right)\int\left[q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}\bar{r}_{1},\chi_{2}\bar{r}_{2}\right)+\chi_{j}\bar{r}_{j}\partial_{j}q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}\bar{r}_{1},\chi_{2}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\right]G_{j}\left(\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2},\rho_{r}^{t}\right)\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\rho_{r}^{t}\right)\int\chi_{\neg j}\bar{r}_{j}\partial_{\neg j}q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}\bar{r}_{1},\chi_{2}\bar{r}_{2}\right)G_{\neg j}\left(\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2},\rho_{r}^{t}\right)\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\lambda\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\int\bar{r}_{j}G_{j}\left(\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2},\rho_{r}^{t}\right)\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\int G_{j}\left(\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2},\rho_{r}^{t}\right)^{2}\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\leq 0.

That is,
E\left(\rho_{r}^{t}\right)
is non-increasing with
t\in[0,\infty)
.
Therefore,
E\left(\rho_{r}^{t}\right)\leq E\left(\rho_{r}^{0}\right)
.
Notice that
\int\left(\bar{r}_{1}^{2}+\bar{r}_{2}^{2}\right){\rm d}\rho_{r}^{0}=r_{0}^{2}\leq C
.
By Lemma
24
,
\left\|q_{j}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C

and hence:

\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\rho_{r}^{0}\right)^{2}\right\}\leq 2\int\bar{r}_{j}^{2}{\rm d}\rho_{r}^{0}+2\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}^{2}\right\}\leq C.

These show that
\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\rho_{r}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}\leq C

for
j=1,2
. Along with Lemma
24
, we
then have:

\displaystyle\left|G_{j}\left(r_{1},r_{2},\rho_{r}^{t}\right)+2\lambda r_{j}\right|
