\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{j}\left(\chi,\rho_{r}^{t}\right)^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)^{2}+\left(\chi_{j}r_{j}\partial_{j}q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)\right)^{2}\right\}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\Delta_{\neg j}\left(\chi,\rho_{r}\right)^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}^{4}/\chi_{\neg j}^{4}\right\}^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\left(\chi_{\neg j}r_{\neg j}\partial_{j}q_{\neg j}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1},\chi_{2}r_{2}\right)\right)^{4}\right\}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq C,

for any
t\geq 0
and any
r_{1},r_{2}\geq 0
.

We now bound
\int\bar{r}_{j}^{p}{\rm d}\rho_{r}^{t}
, for
j=1,2
.
Let
\mathbb{E}_{r}
denote the expectation w.r.t.
\left(r_{1,0},r_{2,0}\right)\sim\rho_{r}^{0}
,
and notice that
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t}\right)
is a deterministic
function of
\left(r_{1,0},r_{2,0}\right)
. We bound the growth of

r_{j,t}
:

r_{j,t}=r_{j,0}+\int_{s=0}^{t}\left(G_{j}\left(r_{1,s},r_{2,s},\rho_{r}^{s}\right)+2\lambda r_{j,s}\right){\rm d}s-2\lambda\int_{s=0}^{t}r_{j,s}{\rm d}s\leq r_{j,0}+Ct,

since
r_{j,s}\geq 0
. This yields:

\int\bar{r}_{j}^{p}{\rm d}\rho_{r}^{t}\leq\mathbb{E}_{r}\left\{\left(r_{j,0}+Ct\right)^{p}\right\}\leq C^{p}\left(\mathbb{E}_{r}\left\{r_{j,0}^{p}\right\}+t^{p}\right)\leq C^{p}\left(p^{p/2}+t^{p}\right)\leq C^{p}\left(1+t^{p}\right)p^{p/2},

giving the desired moment bound.

Next we note that with
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t}\right)=\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,0},r_{2,0}\right)
,

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,0},r_{2,0}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left|\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}r_{j,t}\right|^{2}=\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left|G_{j}\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t},\rho_{r}^{t}\right)\right|^{2}\leq C\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left(1+r_{j,t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+r_{1,t}+r_{2,t}\right)^{2}\leq C\left(1+r_{1,0}+r_{2,0}+t\right)^{2},

as desired.

∎

Proposition 30
.

Consider setting
[S.2
.
Suppose that the initialization
\rho^{0}=\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},r_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{d}/d\right)

for a non-negative constant
r_{0}\leq C
. Given a random vector

\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{0}\sim\rho^{0}
, define the following:

\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}=\left(r_{1,t}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}/\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}\right\|_{2},\quad r_{2,t}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}/\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}\right\|_{2}\right),\qquad\rho^{t}={\rm Law}\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right),

in which
\left(r_{1,t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
and
\left(r_{2,t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

are two non-negative (random) processes, which are independent of

\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}/\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}\right\|_{2}

and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}/\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}\right\|_{2}
,
that are described as in the statement of Theorem
15
.
Then the ODE (
9
) admits
\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t},\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

as a solution. In fact,
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
is the unique
weak solution, and under
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
,
\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

is the unique solution to (
9
).

Proof.

We decompose the proof into several parts. In the following, we let

c_{t}
to be an immaterial positive constant, which may differ at
different instances of use, may depend on time
t
and
\mathfrak{Dim}
,
and is finite with finite
t
. We shall also reuse several quantities
in the description of
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
from
the statement of Theorem
15
. By Proposition

29
, the process
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t},\rho_{r}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

exists and is (weakly) unique. Without loss of generality, let us
assume
r_{1,0}=\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}\right\|_{2}

and
r_{2,0}=\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}\right\|_{2}
.

Verification of the proposed solution.

We first check that the constructed
\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t},\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

is a solution of the ODE (
9
). For brevity, let
\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[j\right]}^{t}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[j\right]}^{t}/\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[j\right]}^{t}\right\|_{2}
,
for
j=1,2
. Firstly since
\rho^{0}=\mathsf{N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},r_{0}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{d}/d\right)
,
we have
r_{1,0}
,
r_{2,0}
,
\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}

and
\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}
are mutually independent.
Furthermore,
\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[1\right]}^{t}=\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}

and
\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[2\right]}^{t}=\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}

for all
t\geq 0
. It is then easy to see from the dynamics of
r_{1,t}

and
r_{2,t}
that
\left(r_{1,t},r_{2,t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
,
\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[1\right]}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

and
\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[2\right]}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
are
mutually independent. Note that
\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[1\right]}^{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}

and
\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[2\right]}^{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}

(where we recall
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\sim\text{Unif}\left(\mathbb{S}^{d_{1}-1}\right)

and
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\sim\text{Unif}\left(\mathbb{S}^{d_{2}-1}\right)

independently), and
r_{1,t}=\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[1\right]}^{t}\right\|_{2}
,

r_{2,t}=\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[2\right]}^{t}\right\|_{2}
.
Using these facts, performing a calculation similar to the proof of
Proposition
25
(in particular, using
Eq. (
28
)), we arrive at the
following:

\displaystyle\nabla_{1}W\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\rho^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla_{1}W\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\rho^{t}\right)_{\left[1\right]},\quad\nabla_{1}W\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\rho^{t}\right)_{\left[2\right]}\right),

\displaystyle\nabla_{1}W\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\rho^{t}\right)_{\left[j\right]}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[j\right]}^{0}\int\bar{r}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\bar{q}_{j}q_{j}^{t}\right\}\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)+\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[j\right]}^{0}r_{j,t}\int\bar{r}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}\bar{q}_{j}\partial_{j}q_{j}^{t}\right\}\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[j\right]}^{0}r_{\neg j,t}\int\bar{r}_{\neg j}\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}\bar{q}_{\neg j}\partial_{j}q_{\neg j}^{t}\right\}\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right),\qquad j=1,2,

Here we have introduced several shortening notations, for
i,j\in\left\{1,2\right\}
:

\bar{q}_{j}=\bar{q}_{j}\left(\chi_{1}\bar{r}_{1},\chi_{2}\bar{r}_{2}\right),\qquad q_{j}^{t}=q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,t},\chi_{2}r_{2,t}\right),\qquad\partial_{i}q_{j}^{t}=\partial_{i}q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}r_{1,t},\chi_{2}r_{2,t}\right).

Next we derive a compatible form of
\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
.
Notice that
\boldsymbol{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\left(\chi_{1}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1},\chi_{2}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\right)

where
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}
,
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}
,
\chi_{1}

and
\chi_{2}
are mutually independent. Therefore,

\displaystyle V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{-\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}+\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}_{\chi,\boldsymbol{\omega}}\left\{\left(\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\kappa\chi_{j}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{j}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\kappa\chi_{j}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{j}\right\rangle\right)\right\}+\lambda\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\chi_{j}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}q_{j}\left(\chi_{1}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2},\chi_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}\right)\right\}+\lambda\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}^{2},

where in the last step, we have performed a calculation similar to
the proof of Proposition
25
(in particular,
we use Eq. (
28
)). This yields:

\displaystyle\nabla V\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla V\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)_{\left[1\right]},\quad\nabla V\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)_{\left[2\right]}\right),

\displaystyle\nabla V\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)_{\left[j\right]}

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}_{\chi}\left\{\chi_{j}q_{j}^{t}+\chi_{j}^{2}r_{j,t}\partial_{j}q_{j}^{t}+\chi_{j}\chi_{\neg j}r_{\neg j,t}\partial_{j}q_{\neg j}^{t}\right\}\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[j\right]}^{0}+2\lambda r_{j,t}\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[j\right]}^{0},\qquad j=1,2.

It is then easy to see that:

\nabla V\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)_{\left[j\right]}+\nabla_{1}W\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\rho^{t}\right)_{\left[j\right]}=-\boldsymbol{u}_{\left[j\right]}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}r_{j,t}=-\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\left[j\right]}^{t},\qquad j=1,2.

Therefore
\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t},\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

is a solution of the ODE (
9
).

Trajectorial uniqueness.

Next we prove that under the given path
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
,
the process
\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

is the unique trajectorial solution to the ODE (
9
) with
initialization
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{0}
. By Proposition
29
,
we have
\int\left(\bar{r}_{1}+\bar{r}_{2}\right)\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\leq c_{t}
,
and hence by Proposition
25
,
\nabla V

and
\nabla_{1}W\left(\cdot;\rho^{t}\right)
are both
c_{t}
-Lipschitz.
A standard argument then yields the desired uniqueness.

Uniqueness in law.

We now prove that
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
is the unique
weak solution with the initialization
\rho^{0}
. Let
\left(\bar{\rho}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

be another solution with the same initialization
\bar{\rho}^{0}=\rho^{0}

(with the equalities holding in the weak sense). We define accordingly
two coupled trajectories
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

and
\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
with the
same initialization
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{0}=\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{0}\sim\rho^{0}
:

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}

\displaystyle=-\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t};\rho^{t}\right),\qquad\rho^{t}={\rm Law}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}

\displaystyle=-\nabla V\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\bar{\rho}^{t}\right),\qquad\bar{\rho}^{t}={\rm Law}\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right).

We examine the distance between these two trajectories:

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}\right)-\nabla V\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t};\rho^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\rho^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\rho^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\bar{\rho}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}.

Define
M_{t}=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}
,
and note that
M_{0}=0
. By Propositions
25
,

28
and
29
,
along with the mean value theorem,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla V\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}\right)-\nabla V\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t};\rho^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\rho^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int\left\|\nabla_{1}U\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-\nabla_{1}U\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right\|_{2}\rho^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{\leq}}\int\left\|\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\rho^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{t}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\int\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}\rho^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{t}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\int\left(\bar{r}_{1}+\bar{r}_{2}\right)\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{t}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}W\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\rho^{t}\right)-\nabla_{1}W\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t};\bar{\rho}^{t}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{=}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\nabla_{1}U\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\right)-\nabla_{1}U\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right)\right\}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\left\|\nabla_{12}^{2}U\left(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq c_{t}\left(1+\left\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq c_{t}\left(1+\left\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\left(1+\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq c_{t}\left(1+\left\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\right)\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{1+\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left\{\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}}+M_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=c_{t}\left(1+\left\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\right)\left(\sqrt{\left(1+\int\left(\bar{r}_{1}^{2}+\bar{r}_{2}^{2}\right)\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)\right)M_{t}}+M_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{t}\left(1+\left\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\right)\left(\sqrt{M_{t}}+M_{t}\right),

where in step
\left(a\right)
,
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{1}\in\left[\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right]
;
in step
\left(b\right)
, we define
\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t},\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right)

and
\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\right)

is independent of
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right)
;
in step
\left(c\right)
,
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}\in\left[\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\right]

and hence
\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\right\|_{2}
.
These bounds imply that

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{t}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+c_{t}\left(1+\left\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\left(\sqrt{M_{t}}+M_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{t}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+c_{t}\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\left(\sqrt{M_{t}}+M_{t}\right).

Taking expectation, by Proposition
29
,
we obtain that for any
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}M_{t}

\displaystyle\leq c_{t}M_{t}+c_{t}\sqrt{\left(1+\int\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}\rho^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)+M_{t}\right)M_{t}}\left(\sqrt{M_{t}}+M_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=c_{t}M_{t}+c_{t}\sqrt{\left(1+\int\left(\bar{r}_{1}^{2}+\bar{r}_{2}^{2}\right)\rho_{r}^{t}\left({\rm d}\bar{r}_{1},{\rm d}\bar{r}_{2}\right)+M_{t}\right)M_{t}}\left(\sqrt{M_{t}}+M_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{t}M_{t}+c_{t}\sqrt{\left(1+M_{t}\right)M_{t}}\left(\sqrt{M_{t}}+M_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{T}M_{t}

for
t\leq T
and
t<t_{*}
with
t_{*}=\inf\left\{t\geq 0:\;M_{t}>1\right\}
.
Since
M_{0}=0
and
M_{t}\geq 0
, Gronwall’s lemma then implies
that
t_{*}>T
and
M_{t}=0
for all
t\leq T
. Since this is satisfied
for any
T\geq 0
, we have
M_{t}=0
for all
t\geq 0
. Note that

M_{t}=0
implies, for any
1
-Lipschitz test function
\phi:\;\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\left|\int\phi\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\bar{\rho}^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-\int\phi\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\rho^{t}\left({\rm d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right|\leq\inf_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{a}\sim\bar{\rho}^{t},\;\boldsymbol{\theta}_{b}\sim\rho^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{a}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{b}\right\|_{2}\right\}\leq\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{t}\right\|_{2}\right\}\leq\sqrt{M_{t}}=0.

This proves weak uniqueness of the solution
\left(\rho^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

with initialization
\rho^{0}
.

∎

Proposition 31
.

Consider setting
[S.2
.
For a collection of vectors
\Theta=\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)_{i\leq N}

where
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\boldsymbol{x}\sim{\cal P}

and
\boldsymbol{z}=\left(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{x}\right)
,
we have
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)
is
sub-exponential with
\psi_{1}
-norm:

\left\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\kappa^{2}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}+1\right)\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}}+1\right).

Proof.

Consider a fixed vector
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
:

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\nabla_{2}\sigma_{*}\left(\boldsymbol{x};\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)^{\top}\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{y}}_{N}\left(\boldsymbol{x};\Theta\right)-\boldsymbol{x}\right)\right\rangle+\lambda\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\nabla_{1}\Lambda\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)+\kappa^{2}\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle+2\lambda\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3},

where we denote
\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}=\left(1/N\right)\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{N}\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)

for brevity. We examine each component in the above:

•

Since
\left\|\sigma\right\|_{\infty}\leq C
, for any
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle

is sub-Gaussian with
\psi_{2}
-norm

\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\frac{\kappa}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\right\rangle\right|\leq C\kappa\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{2}.

We have
\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle

is sub-Gaussian with
\psi_{2}
-norm
\left\|\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}=\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}
.
Therefore,
A_{1}
is sub-Gaussian:

\left\|A_{1}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\kappa\left(\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}+\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\right)\leq C\kappa\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{2}+1\right).

•

Since
\left\|\sigma^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C
,
A_{2}
is
sub-exponential:

\displaystyle\left\|A_{2}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa\left(\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}+\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\right)\left\|\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa\left(\kappa\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\kappa}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\right)\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{2}+1\right).

•

A_{3}
is a constant and so it is sub-exponential with
\psi_{1}
-norm

\left\|A_{3}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}
.

We have
\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\rangle

and hence
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)

are sub-exponential:

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\left(\Theta;\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\kappa^{2}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}+1\right)\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{2}+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}+1\right)\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}}+1\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 32
.

Consider setting
[S.2
.
Let
\rho={\rm Law}\left(r_{1}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1},r_{2}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\right)

in which
\left(r_{1},r_{2}\right)
,
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}
and

\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}
are mutually independent and
\left(r_{1},r_{2}\right)\sim\rho_{r}

such that
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
are non-negative and marginally
C
-sub-Gaussian.
We have, for some sufficiently large
C_{*}
, with probability at
least
1-C\exp\left(Cd-CN/\kappa^{4}\right)
,

\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C_{*},

in which
\boldsymbol{\zeta}
is a fixed vector with
\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}<\infty
,
and
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)_{i\leq N}\sim_{{\rm i.i.d.}}\rho
.
Here
C_{*}
does not depend on
d
or
N
.

Proof.

We proceed in a fashion similar to the proof of Lemma
21
.
Let us decompose

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)=\boldsymbol{M}_{1}+\boldsymbol{M}_{1}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d},

for which

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\kappa^{3}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}.

Below we bound
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
separately.

Step 1: Bounding
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.

For a given
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let us define
\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\equiv\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\left(\boldsymbol{x}\right)

as in the statement of Proposition
25
.
Let us also define the quantity
A_{1}=\kappa^{2}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}\right\|_{2}
.
We observe that for any
\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\right|=\kappa^{2}\left|\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\kappa\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left|\kappa\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right|^{2}\right\}}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2},

by Proposition
25
and the fact
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C
,
and therefore
A_{1}\leq C
. Furthermore, we have:

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}-A_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}-\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)-\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\right]\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}\right\}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\equiv\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}.

Here we making the following claim:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\geq\delta\right\}\leq C\exp\left(Cd-C\delta^{2}N/\kappa^{4}\right),

for
\delta\geq 0
. Assuming this claim, we thus have for
\delta\geq 0

and some sufficiently large
C^{\prime}
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\geq C^{\prime}+\delta\right\}\leq C\exp\left(Cd-C\delta^{2}N/\kappa^{4}\right),

which is the desired result.

We are left with proving the claim on
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.
Given fixed
\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}},\qquad M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}=\kappa\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)-\hat{\boldsymbol{x}},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right\}.

First notice that
\left(M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right)_{i\leq N}

are i.i.d. Furthermore
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\right\}=\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}

by Proposition
25
. Therefore
\mathbb{E}\left\{M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right\}=0
.
For any positive integer
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\left|\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)-\hat{\boldsymbol{x}},\kappa\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right\}\right|^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{\leq}}C^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)-\hat{\boldsymbol{x}},\kappa\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle^{2}\right\}^{p/2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle^{2}\right\}^{p/2}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{\leq}}C^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\kappa^{2}\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle^{2}+\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{x}},\kappa\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle^{2}\right\}^{p/2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{x},\kappa\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle^{2}\right\}^{p/2}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{\leq}}C^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\left(\kappa^{2}\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle^{2}+\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{p/2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(d\right)}}{{\leq}}C^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\kappa^{2p}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{p}+1\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(e\right)}}{{\leq}}C^{p}\left(\kappa^{2p}\int\left(r_{1}^{p}+r_{2}^{p}\right){\rm d}\rho_{r}+1\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(f\right)}}{{\leq}}C^{p}\left(\kappa^{2p}p^{p/2}+1\right),

where we have use the fact that
\left\|\sigma\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\sigma^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C

in steps
\left(a\right)
and
\left(b\right)
,
\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{x}},\kappa\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle^{2}\right\}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}^{2}

by Proposition
25
in step
\left(c\right)
,

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C
and

\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}=1

in step
\left(d\right)
,
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\left(r_{1}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1},r_{2}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\right)

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\right\|_{2}=1

in step
\left(e\right)
, and
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
are
C
-sub-Gaussian
in step
\left(f\right)
. It is easy to see that
M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}

is a sub-Gaussian random variable with
\psi_{2}
-norm
\left\|M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\kappa^{2}
.
Then by Lemma
34
, for any
\delta>0
,
with probability at most
C\exp\left(-C\delta^{2}N/\kappa^{4}\right)
,

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|=\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}M_{1,1,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right|\geq\delta.

Now we construct an epsilon-net
{\cal N}\subset\mathbb{S}^{d-1}

such that for any
\boldsymbol{a}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, there exists

\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}\in{\cal N}
with
\left\|\boldsymbol{a}-\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\leq 1/3
.
There is such an epsilon-net
{\cal N}
with size
\left|{\cal N}\right|\leq 9^{d}

[
Ver10
]
. A standard argument yields

\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq 3\max_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\in{\cal N}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle.

Therefore, by the union bound, we obtain:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\geq\delta\right\}\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\in{\cal N}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\geq\delta/3\right\}\leq C\exp\left(Cd-C\delta^{2}N/\kappa^{4}\right).

This proves the claim.

Step 2: Bounding
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.

Given fixed
\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}},\qquad M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}=\kappa\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta},\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right\},

where
\boldsymbol{z}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)
.
First we bound
\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right|^{p}\right\}

for an integer
p\geq 1
. We note that for
w=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)
,

\left(w,\boldsymbol{z}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\left(w,{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right),

for
\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)

independent of
w
. Therefore, letting
\boldsymbol{S}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}^{\perp}

for brevity, we obtain:

\displaystyle M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}

\displaystyle=\kappa\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\Bigg\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta},\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle+\frac{w\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigg[\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle+\frac{w^{2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{4}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\Bigg]\Bigg\}.

Using Lemma
27
along with the facts

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}\geq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}
,

\left\|\boldsymbol{S}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}=1
,
we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\Bigg\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta},\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\rangle\right|\Bigg[\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}}+\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{3}}\Bigg]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right\|_{2}.

Therefore,
\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right|^{p}\right\}\leq C\kappa^{2p}\int\left(r_{1}^{p}+r_{2}^{p}\right){\rm d}\rho_{r}\leq C\kappa^{2p}p^{p/2}
.
That is,
M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}
is
C\kappa^{2}
-sub-Gaussian.

The above bound, however, does not give a satisfactory bound for the
quantity
\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right\}\right|=\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right\}\right|
,
since it incurs a factor
\kappa^{2}
in the bound. We give a more
careful treatment of this quantity here. By Proposition
25
:

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left\{\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right\rangle+\frac{w\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\right\}=\left(s_{1}\hat{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}_{\left[1\right]},\;s_{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}_{\left[2\right]}\right)

in which we define

\hat{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}=\frac{1}{\kappa}\left(\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\right),\qquad s_{j}=\int\frac{r_{j}}{\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}_{\left[j\right]}\right\|_{2}}q_{j}\left(\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}_{\left[1\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{1},\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}_{\left[2\right]}\right\|_{2}r_{2}\right)\rho_{r}\left({\rm d}r_{1},{\rm d}r_{2}\right),\qquad j=1,2,

and
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
are defined in (
21
)
and (
22
). This yields the formula:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{w,\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\Bigg\{\left(\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}s_{j}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\left[j\right]},\left(\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right)_{\left[j\right]}\right\rangle+s_{j}w\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\left[j\right]},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(w\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigg[\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{w}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle+\frac{w^{2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{4}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\Bigg]\Bigg\}.

By Lemma
24
and the fact
\int\left(r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}\right){\rm d}\rho_{r}\leq C
,
we have
\left|s_{1}\right|,\left|s_{2}\right|\leq C
. Then applying
Lemma
27
along with the facts
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}\geq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}
,

\left\|\boldsymbol{S}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\boldsymbol{v}\right\|_{2}=1
,
we obtain:

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\Bigg\{\left(\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\left[j\right]},\left(\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\right)_{\left[j\right]}\right\rangle\right|+\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\left[j\right]},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\left[j\right]}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigg[\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}}+\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{u}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{3}}\Bigg]\Bigg\}\leq C.

Let this upper-bounding constant be
C_{1}
.

To complete the present step, notice that
\left(M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right)_{i\leq N}

are i.i.d. Then by Lemma
34
, for
any
\delta>0
, with probability at most
C\exp\left(-C\delta^{2}N/\kappa^{4}\right)
,

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle-\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right\}\right|=\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}-\mathbb{E}\left\{M_{2,i}^{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}}\right\}\right|\geq\delta,

which also implies

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right|\geq\delta-\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right\}\right|\geq\delta-C_{1},

since
\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right\}\right|\leq C_{1}
.
We opt for
\delta=2C_{1}
. Now we can reuse the same epsilon-net
argument in the analysis of
\boldsymbol{M}_{1,1}
to obtain:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\geq C_{1}\right\}\leq C\exp\left(Cd-CC_{1}N/\kappa^{4}\right).

Step 3: Putting all together.

From the bounds on
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{2}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\geq C_{*}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(Cd-CN/\kappa^{4}\right),

for sufficiently large
C_{*}
. This completes the proof.

∎

Proposition 33
.

Consider setting
[S.2
.
For each integer
i=1,...,N
, we draw independently
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1,i}\sim{\rm Unif}\left(\mathbb{S}^{d_{1}-1}\right)
,

\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2,i}\sim{\rm Unif}\left(\mathbb{S}^{d_{2}-1}\right)
,

r_{1,i}
and
r_{2,i}
, with
r_{1,i}
and
r_{2,i}
being non-negative

C
-sub-Gaussian random variables. Let
\left(\psi_{t}\right)_{t\in\left[0,T\right]}

be a collection of (deterministic) functions, which map from
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

to
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, such that:

•

for any
t\in\left[0,T\right]
, each of the two entries in
\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)

is marginally
C
-sub-Gaussian,

•

\left\|\partial_{t}\psi_{t}\left(r_{1},r_{2}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\left(1+r_{1}+r_{2}\right)

for any
t\in\left[0,T\right]
and
\psi_{0}\left(r_{1},r_{2}\right)=\left(r_{1},r_{2}\right)
.

For each
i\leq N
and
t\in\left[0,T\right]
, we form
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}=\left(\left(\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)\right)_{1}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{1,i},\;\left(\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)\right)_{2}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2,i}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
where
\left(\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)\right)_{j}
denotes
the
j
-th entry of
\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)
, for

j=1,2
. Then for any
c>0
and
T>0
, with probability at least

1-C\exp\left(Cd\log\left(\kappa^{2}\sqrt{N}+e\right)-CN/\kappa^{4}\right)
,

\sup_{t\in\left[0,T\right]}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in{\cal B}_{d}\left(c\sqrt{N}\right)}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C_{*},

for some sufficiently large constant
C_{*}
. (The constants
C

and
C_{*}
do not depend on
d
or
N
, may depend on
c
and

T
and are finite with finite
c
and
T
.)

Proof.

The proof leverages on Lemma
32
and comprises
of several steps. Without loss of generality, let us assume
c=T=1
.
That is, we shall study the quantity

Q=\sup_{t\in\left[0,1\right]}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in{\cal B}_{d}\left(\sqrt{N}\right)}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}.

Step 1: Epsilon-net argument.

Fix
\gamma\in\left(0,1/3\right)
. Consider an epsilon-net
{\cal N}_{d}\left(\gamma\right)\subset{\cal B}_{d}\left(\sqrt{N}\right)

in which for any
\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in{\cal B}_{d}\left(\sqrt{N}\right)
,
there exists
\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\in{\cal N}_{d}\left(\gamma\right)

such that
\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\leq\gamma\sqrt{N}
.
A standard volumetric argument
[
Ver10
]
shows
that there exists such epsilon-net with size
\left|{\cal N}_{d}\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq\left(3/\gamma\right)^{d}
.
Likewise let
{\cal N}\left(\gamma\right)=\left\{k\gamma:\;k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0},\;0\leq k\gamma\leq 1\right\}
,
and note that
\left|{\cal N}\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1+1/\gamma
.
Consider
t\in\left[0,1\right]
and
t^{\prime}\in{\cal N}\left(\gamma\right)

such that
\left|t-t^{\prime}\right|\leq\gamma
, and
\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in{\cal B}_{d}\left(\sqrt{N}\right)

and
\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\in{\cal N}_{d}\left(\gamma\right)
such
that
\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\leq\gamma\sqrt{N}
.
We have:

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left|\left(\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)\right)_{j}-\left(\psi_{t^{\prime}}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)\right)_{j}\right|\leq 2\sup_{s\in\left[t,t^{\prime}\right]}\left\|\partial_{s}\psi_{s}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)\right\|_{2}\left|t-t^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(r_{1,i}+r_{2,i}+1\right)\gamma.

Furthermore, for any
t\in\left[0,T\right]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left|\left(\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)\right)_{j}\right|=\sum_{j\in\left\{1,2\right\}}\left|\left(\psi_{0}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)\right)_{j}+\int_{s=0}^{t}\partial_{s}\left(\psi_{s}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)\right)_{j}{\rm d}s\right|

\displaystyle\leq r_{1,i}+r_{2,i}+C\int_{s=0}^{t}\left(r_{1,i}+r_{2,i}+1\right){\rm d}s\leq C\left(r_{1,i}+r_{2,i}+1\right).

We then have from the mean value theorem:

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}-\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t^{\prime}}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right)-\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}+\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right)-\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t^{\prime}}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\nabla_{111}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\nabla_{121}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\nabla_{111}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}}\gamma\sqrt{N}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}C\kappa^{2}\left(1+\left\|\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\right\|_{2}\right)\left(r_{1,i}+r_{2,i}+1\right)\gamma

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\nabla_{111}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right]\right\|_{{\rm op}}\gamma\sqrt{N}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}C\kappa^{2}\left(r_{1,i}^{2}+r_{2,i}^{2}+1\right)\gamma,

where in step
\left(a\right)
, we have
\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\in\left[\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\prime}\right]

and
\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\in\left[\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t^{\prime}}\right]
;
in step
\left(b\right)
, we apply Proposition
28
;
in step
\left(c\right)
, we use the fact that
\left\|\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t^{\prime}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}
.
We have:

\nabla_{111}^{3}U\left[\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right]=\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}+\boldsymbol{M}_{2,i}+\boldsymbol{M}_{3,i}+\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)^{\otimes 3},

for which

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}

\displaystyle=K\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\right\},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{2,i}

\displaystyle=K\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{3,i}

\displaystyle=K\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\right\},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{5}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}\right\}.

Note that
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}=\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{2,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}=\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{3,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.
We then have:

\left|Q-Q_{\gamma}\right|\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(3\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}+\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\right)\gamma\sqrt{N}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}C\kappa^{2}\left(r_{1,i}^{2}+r_{2,i}^{2}+1\right)\gamma,

(32)

in which we define:

Q_{\gamma}=\max_{t\in{\cal N}\left(\gamma\right)}\max_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in{\cal N}_{d}\left(\gamma\right)}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{11}^{2}U\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right)\right\|_{{\rm op}}.

The next two steps are devoted to bounding
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.

Step 2: Bounding
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.

To bound
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
,
we have for any
\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{c}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i},\boldsymbol{a}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}\otimes\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa^{4}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left\{\sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\right\},

where
\boldsymbol{z}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)
.
Recalling that
\left\|\sigma\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\sigma^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{2}\leq C
, we thus
have:

\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i},\boldsymbol{a}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}\otimes\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle\right|\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2}.

That is,
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\|_{2}
.

Step 3: Bounding
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
.

Notice that for
w_{i}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{z}\right\rangle\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)
,

\left(w_{i},\boldsymbol{z}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm d}}}{{=}}\left(w_{i},{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+\frac{w_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right),

in which
\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}\sim\mathsf{N}\left(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)

independent of
w_{i}
. We then have:

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i},\boldsymbol{a}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}\otimes\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\cal P}}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\kappa\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{x}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{w_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\Bigg\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\rangle\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(w_{i}\right)\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t},\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}+w_{i}\frac{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigg[\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{a}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{b}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle+w_{i}\sum_{\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2},\boldsymbol{v}_{3}\right)}\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\right\rangle\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{v}_{3}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+w_{i}^{2}\sum_{\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2},\boldsymbol{v}_{3}\right)}\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\right\rangle\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{v}_{3}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}+w_{i}^{3}\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{a}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{b}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\bigg]\Bigg\},

where
\boldsymbol{S}_{i}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}{\rm Proj}_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}^{\perp}

for brevity and the summations are over
\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2},\boldsymbol{v}_{3}\in\left\{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{c}\right\}

with
\boldsymbol{v}_{1}
,
\boldsymbol{v}_{2}
,
\boldsymbol{v}_{3}

being mutually different. Then by Lemma
27
,
along with the facts
\left\|\sigma\right\|_{\infty}\leq C
,

\left\|\boldsymbol{S}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}\geq C\left\|\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}
,
we have:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i},\boldsymbol{a}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}\otimes\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}\Bigg\{\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}}\Bigg[\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{a}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{b}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle\right|+\sum_{\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2},\boldsymbol{v}_{3}\right)}\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\right\rangle\right|\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{v}_{3}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\left(\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2},\boldsymbol{v}_{3}\right)}\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{S}\tilde{\boldsymbol{z}},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\right\rangle\right|\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}}\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{v}_{3}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}}+\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{a}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}}\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{b}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}}\frac{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{i},\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}}\bigg]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{a}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{c}\right\|_{2}.

That is,
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C\kappa^{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\|_{2}
.

Step 4: Finishing the proof.

From the bounds on
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{1,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

and
\left\|\boldsymbol{M}_{4,i}\right\|_{{\rm op}}
and
Eq. (
32
) , we get:

\displaystyle\left|Q-Q_{\gamma}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\kappa^{2}\gamma\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{t}\right\|_{2}\sqrt{N}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}C\kappa^{2}\left(r_{1,i}^{2}+r_{2,i}^{2}+1\right)\gamma

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\kappa^{2}\gamma\left(r_{1,i}+r_{2,i}+1\right)\sqrt{N}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}C\kappa^{2}\left(r_{1,i}^{2}+r_{2,i}^{2}+1\right)\gamma\leq C\kappa^{2}\gamma\left(\sqrt{NA}+A+\sqrt{N}\right),

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(r_{1,i}^{2}+r_{2,i}^{2}\right).

Recall that
r_{1,i}^{2}+r_{2,i}^{2}
is
C
-sub-exponential. Then
by Lemma
34
, for
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{A\geq C_{1}\left(1+\delta\right)\right\}\leq C\exp\left(-CN\delta^{2}\right)
,
where
C_{1}=\int\left(r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}\right){\rm d}\rho_{r}\leq C
.
Furthermore, since
\left(\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)\right)_{1}

and
\left(\psi_{t}\left(r_{1,i},r_{2,i}\right)\right)_{2}
are
C
-sub-Gaussian,
using Lemma
32
and the union bound, we obtain
for sufficiently large
C_{*}
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{Q_{\gamma}\geq C_{*}\right\}\leq\left|{\cal N}_{d}\left(\gamma\right)\right|\left|{\cal N}\left(\gamma\right)\right|C\exp\left(Cd-CN/\kappa^{4}\right)\leq\left(\frac{3}{\gamma}\right)^{d+1}C\exp\left(Cd-CN/\kappa^{4}\right).

Let us choose
\gamma=1/\left(4\kappa^{2}\sqrt{N}\right)<1/3
and

\delta=0.5
. Then for sufficiently large
C_{*}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{Q\geq C_{*}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(-CN\right)+\left(C\kappa^{2}\sqrt{N}\right)^{d+1}C\exp\left(Cd-CN/\kappa^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(Cd\log\left(\kappa^{2}\sqrt{N}+e\right)-CN/\kappa^{4}\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix A
Technical lemmas

A.1
Sub-Gaussian and sub-exponential random variables

We recall the Orlicz norms for a real-valued random variable
X
:

\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{2}}=\sup_{p\geq 1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|X\right|^{p}\right\}^{1/p},\qquad\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{1}}=\sup_{p\geq 1}\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|X\right|^{p}\right\}^{1/p}.

A real-valued random variable
X
is
K
-sub-Gaussian if
K=\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{2}}

is finite. It is
K
-sub-exponential if
K=\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{1}}

is finite. A random vector
\boldsymbol{X}
is
K
-sub-Gaussian
if
\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle
is
sub-Gaussian for any
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and in
particular,
K=\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}<\infty
.

We summarize the following well-known facts about sub-Gaussian and
sub-exponential random variables
[
Ver10
]
:

Lemma 34
.

The following properties hold:

•

X
is
K
-sub-Gaussian if and only if there exists a constant
K_{0}

that differs from
K
by at most an absolute constant factor, such
that
\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|X\right|>t\right\}\leq\exp\left(1-t^{2}/K_{0}^{2}\right)

for all
t\geq 0
.

•

X
is
K
-sub-exponential if and only if there exists a constant

K_{0}
that differs from
K
by at most an absolute constant factor,
such that
\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|X\right|>t\right\}\leq\exp\left(1-t/K_{0}\right)

for all
t\geq 0
.

•

For two sub-Gaussian random variables
X
and
Y
, their sum
X+Y

is sub-Gaussian with
\psi_{2}
-norm
\left\|X+Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{2}}+\left\|Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}
.
Likewise, if they are sub-exponential, their sum is sub-exponential
with norm
\left\|X+Y\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{1}}+\left\|Y\right\|_{\psi_{1}}
.

•

For two sub-Gaussian random variables
X
and
Y
, their product

XY
is sub-exponential with
\psi_{1}
-norm
\left\|XY\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\left\|Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}
.

•

If
X
is sub-exponential with zero mean and
\left\|X\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq K
,
then for any
t
such that
\left|t\right|\leq c/K
,
\mathbb{E}\left\{e^{tX}\right\}\leq e^{Ct^{2}K^{2}}

for some absolute constants
C,c>0
.

•

Let
X_{1},...,X_{n}
be independent sub-Gaussian random variables
with zero mean, and let
K=\max_{i\in\left[n\right]}\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}
.
Then for any
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right|\geq tn\right\}\leq e\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{cnt^{2}}{K^{2}}\right),

for an absolute constant
c>0
.

•

Let
X_{1},...,X_{n}
be independent sub-exponential random variables
with zero mean, and let
K=\max_{i\in\left[n\right]}\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}
.
Then for any
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right|\geq tn\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-cn\min\left(\frac{t^{2}}{K^{2}},\frac{t}{K}\right)\right),

for an absolute constant
c>0
.

We also have the following martingale concentration result for sub-exponential
martingale difference:

Lemma 35
.

Let
\left(X^{k}\right)_{k\geq 0}
be a real-valued
martingale w.r.t. the filtration
\left({\cal F}^{k}\right)_{k\geq 0}

with
X^{0}=0
. Suppose that the martingale difference
X^{k}-X^{k-1}
,
conditioned on
{\cal F}^{k-1}
, is
K
-sub-exponential with zero
mean. Then:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{k\leq n}\left|X^{k}\right|\geq c_{1}K\sqrt{n}\delta\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-\delta^{2}\right),

for
\delta\leq c_{2}\sqrt{n}
, for some
c_{1},c_{2}>0
absolute
constants.

Proof.

We have for
t>0
and
t
such that
\left|t\right|\leq c/K
,

\mathbb{E}\left\{e^{t\left(X^{k}-X^{k-1}\right)}\middle|{\cal F}^{k-1}\right\}\leq e^{Ct^{2}K^{2}},

for some absolute constants
C,c>0
by Lemma
34
.
This results in the recursive relation:

\mathbb{E}\left\{e^{tX^{k}}\right\}=\mathbb{E}\left\{e^{tX^{k-1}}\mathbb{E}\left\{e^{t\left(X^{k}-X^{k-1}\right)}\middle|{\cal F}^{k-1}\right\}\right\}\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{e^{tX^{k-1}}\right\}e^{Ct^{2}K^{2}},

which implies

\mathbb{E}\left\{e^{tX^{n}}\right\}\leq e^{Ct^{2}K^{2}n}.

A standard argument yields a tail bound on
\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|X_{n}\right|\geq n\delta\right\}
.
In particular, by Markov’s inequality, for
\delta>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{X^{n}\geq n\delta\right\}\leq\inf_{t\in\left[0,c/K\right]}e^{-n\delta t}\mathbb{E}\left\{e^{tX^{n}}\right\}\leq\inf_{t\in\left[0,c/K\right]}e^{Ct^{2}K^{2}n-n\delta t}\leq\exp\left(-n\min\left(\frac{\delta^{2}}{4CK^{2}},\frac{c\delta}{K}\right)\right).

The same argument yields the same bound for
\mathbb{P}\left\{-X_{n}\geq n\delta\right\}
.
Then:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|X^{n}\right|\geq n\delta\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-n\min\left(\frac{\delta^{2}}{4CK^{2}},\frac{c\delta}{K}\right)\right).

Define the stopping time
T=\min\left\{k:\;\left|X^{k}\right|\geq n\delta\right\}

and the martingale
\bar{X}^{k}=X^{k\wedge T}
. Since
\max_{k\leq n}\left|X^{k}\right|\geq n\delta

if and only if
\bar{X}^{n}\geq n\delta
, the same bound applies
to
\max_{k\leq n}\left|X^{k}\right|
. Finally, defining
z=\sqrt{n\delta^{2}/\left(4CK^{2}\right)}
,
for
z\leq\sqrt{4nc^{2}C}
, we have:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{k\leq n}\left|X^{k}\right|\geq\sqrt{4CK^{2}n}z\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-z^{2}\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

The following lemma provides an estimate on the expected norm of sub-exponential
random vector:

Lemma 36
.

Let
\boldsymbol{X}
be a sub-exponential
random vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq K

and
\mathbb{E}\left\{\boldsymbol{X}\right\}=\boldsymbol{0}
. Then
for some sufficiently large constant
C
that does not depend on

d
or
K
,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\leq C\left(d^{2}K^{2}+1\right).

Proof.

To compute
\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}
,
we first provide a tail bound on
\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}\geq\delta\right\}
.
Consider an epsilon-net
{\cal N}\subset\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
such that
for any
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, there exists
\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\in{\cal N}

with
\left\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\leq 1/2
.
There exists such an epsilon-net
[
Ver10
]

with size
\left|{\cal N}\right|\leq 6^{d}
. For
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,
let
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\in{\cal N}

be such that
\left\|\boldsymbol{u}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq 1/2
.
Then:

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right),\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle+\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right),\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle+\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in{\cal N}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}+\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in{\cal N}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle,

and hence
\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}\leq 2\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in{\cal N}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle
.
Now fix a vector
\boldsymbol{u}\in{\cal N}
. Since
\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle

has zero mean and
\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq K
,
by Lemma
34
, for any
t
such
that
\left|t\right|\leq c_{1}/K
,
\mathbb{E}\left\{e^{t\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle}\right\}\leq e^{c_{2}t^{2}K^{2}}

for some absolute constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
. By Markov’s inequality,
for
\delta\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle\geq\delta\right\}\leq\inf_{t\in\left[0,c_{1}/K\right]}e^{-\delta t}\mathbb{E}\left\{e^{t\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle}\right\}\leq\inf_{t\in\left[0,c_{1}/K\right]}e^{c_{2}t^{2}K^{2}-\delta t}\leq\exp\left(-\min\left(\frac{\delta^{2}}{4c_{2}K^{2}},\frac{c_{1}\delta}{K}\right)\right).

The same argument yields the same bound for
\mathbb{P}\left\{-\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle\geq\delta\right\}
.
Then:

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle\right|\geq\delta\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-\min\left(\frac{\delta^{2}}{4c_{2}K^{2}},\frac{c_{1}\delta}{K}\right)\right).

By the union bound,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}\geq\delta\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(d\log 6-\min\left(\frac{\delta^{2}}{16c_{2}K^{2}},\frac{c_{1}\delta}{2K}\right)\right).

Now to compute
\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}
,
observe that firstly
\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}\geq\delta\right\}\leq 1

trivially, and secondly, if
\delta\geq 8\left(c_{3}dK\log 6\right)/c_{1}

for
c_{3}\geq\max\left\{1,c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\log 6\right\}\geq\left(c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\log 6\right)/d
,
then we have

\min\left(\frac{\delta^{2}}{16c_{2}K^{2}},\frac{c_{1}\delta}{2K}\right)=\frac{c_{1}\delta}{2K},\qquad d\log 6-\frac{c_{1}\delta}{2K}\leq-\frac{3c_{1}\delta}{8K}.

Therefore we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq t\right\}{\rm d}t=2\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{X}\right\|_{2}\geq\delta\right\}\delta{\rm d}\delta

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{8\left(c_{3}dK\log 6\right)/c_{1}}\delta{\rm d}\delta+4\int_{8\left(c_{3}dK\log 6\right)/c_{1}}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{3c_{1}\delta}{8K}\right)\delta{\rm d}\delta

\displaystyle=\frac{64c_{3}^{2}d^{2}K^{2}\log^{2}6}{c_{1}^{2}}+\frac{256K^{2}}{9c_{1}^{2}}\left(3c_{3}d\log 6+1\right)e^{-3c_{3}d\log 6}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(d^{2}K^{2}+1\right),

for some sufficiently large
C
that depends only on
c_{1}
and

c_{3}
.
∎

A.2
Moment controls

We have the following control on the moments of the norm of the average
of (almost) independent random vectors:

Lemma 37
.

Consider a random variable
X
and a
sequence of random vectors
\left(\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{X}\right)_{j\leq N}
.
Assume
\left(\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{X}\right)_{j\leq N}
are independent
conditionally on
X
,
\mathbb{E}\left\{\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{X}\middle|X\right\}=\boldsymbol{0}
,
and
\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{X}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq K

for all
j\in\left[N\right]
, for some positive integer
p
and
constant
K
. Then:

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{a}_{i}^{X}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq 4^{p}\left(2p\right)!\frac{K}{N^{p}}\leq 16^{p}p^{2p}\frac{K}{N^{p}}.

In fact, the same statement holds for
\left(\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{X}\right)_{j\leq N}

defined on a Hilbert space, equipped with an inner product
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle

and an induced norm
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{2}
.

Proof.

We use a symmetrization argument. Define
\left(\varepsilon_{j}\right)_{j\leq N}

being i.i.d. Bernoulli
\pm 1
random variables, independent of everything
else. Since
\mathbb{E}\left\{\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{X}\middle|X\right\}=\boldsymbol{0}

and
\left(\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{X}\right)_{j\leq N}
are independent
conditionally on
X
, we have the following symmetrization fact
[
LT13
, Lemma 6.3]
:

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{X}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq 4^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{b}_{j}^{X}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\},

(33)

in which
\boldsymbol{b}_{j}^{X}=\varepsilon_{j}\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{X}
.
We note that
\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{b}_{j}^{X}\right\|_{2}^{2p}

is a sum of
N^{2p}
terms of the form
\prod_{h=1}^{p}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{h},\boldsymbol{b}_{2h}\right\rangle
,
where
\boldsymbol{b}_{h}\in\left\{\boldsymbol{b}_{j}^{X}\right\}_{j\leq N}

for
h=1,...,2p
. Consider a term
H
that has
q_{j}
appearances
of
\boldsymbol{b}_{j}^{X}
for
j\in J_{H}\subseteq\left[N\right]
,
where
\sum_{j\in J_{H}}q_{j}=2p
. We have by Holder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{H\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{\prod_{j\in J_{H}}\left\|\boldsymbol{b}_{j}^{X}\right\|_{2}^{q_{j}}\right\}\leq\prod_{j\in J_{H}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{b}_{j}^{X}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}^{q_{j}/\left(2p\right)}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j\in J_{H}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{X}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}^{q_{j}/\left(2p\right)}\leq\prod_{j\in J_{H}}K^{q_{j}/\left(2p\right)}=K.

Notice that the above upper bound is the same for all terms. Furthermore
if there is
j\in J_{H}
such that
q_{j}
is odd, then
\mathbb{E}\left\{H\middle|X\right\}=0
,
thanks to the randomness of
\varepsilon_{j}
. Hence we only need
to upper bound the number of terms
H
such that there is no
j\in J_{H}

with odd
q_{j}
. Let us call this number
N_{*}
. To bound
N_{*}
,
we consider the following construction of each desired term. As the
first step, we select
\boldsymbol{b}_{h}
from the set
\left\{\boldsymbol{b}_{j}^{X}\right\}_{j\leq N}

for
h=1,...,p
, and we set
\boldsymbol{b}_{2h}=\boldsymbol{b}_{h}
.
Then in the second step, we construct the desired term as
\prod_{h=1}^{p}\left\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{\Pi\left(h\right)},\boldsymbol{b}_{\Pi\left(2h\right)}\right\rangle
,
where
\Pi:\;\left[2p\right]\to\left[2p\right]
is any permutation.
This procedure guarantees to construct all desired terms, with some
being repeated. Note that the number of possibilities for the first
step is
N^{p}
, and in the second step, the number of permutations
is
\left(2p\right)!
. Hence we obtain
N_{*}\leq\left(2p\right)!N^{p}
.
Therefore, by Eq. (
33
),

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{X}\right\|_{2}^{2p}\right\}\leq 4^{p}\left(2p\right)!KN^{p},

which completes the proof.
∎

The above result presents a simple approach to concentration for powers
of sub-Gaussian random variables:

Lemma 38
.

Let
\left(X_{i}\right)_{i\geq 0}

be independent real-valued
K
-sub-Gaussian random variables. Then
for any
q\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|X_{i}\right|^{q}-\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|X_{i}\right|^{q}\right\}\right|\geq\delta\right\}\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{C^{1/\left(2+q\right)}N^{1/\left(2+q\right)}\delta^{2/\left(2+q\right)}}{K^{2q/\left(2+q\right)}}\right),

where the constant
C
does not depend on
q
or
K
.

Proof.

Let
Y_{i}=\left|X_{i}\right|^{q}-\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|X_{i}\right|^{q}\right\}

and
S=\left(1/N\right)\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i}
. We have for any
positive integer
p
,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|Y_{i}\right|^{2p}\right\}\leq 4^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|X_{i}\right|^{2pq}\right\}\leq 4^{p}K^{2pq}p^{pq}.

By Lemma
37
,
